CBSE Class 10 Maths
Mathematics develops a solid foundation for higher-level reasoning and problem-solving abilities, and the CBSE class 10 curriculum is designed to help students master fundamental concepts useful in solving advanced-level problems from CBSE class 10 Maths chapters.
The CBSE curriculum helps students explore mathematical concepts in depth and apply them in real-life situations, which ensures a better understanding of the subject. Each topic is taught according to its practical application in real life.
NCERT CBSE Class 10 Maths Syllabus has been provided to assist students in understanding all the chapters and preparing their study plans. This blog will cover several aspects of the CBSE Class 10 Maths Syllabus, including chapter-wise weightage and preparation tips.
Students can also download the ALLEN's App to enhance their learning experiences significantly. It allows students to acquire new information more quickly and efficiently. Click here to understand the advantages of the ALLEN app.
1.0CBSE Class 10 Maths Syllabus
The syllabus of CBSE Class 10 Maths is given below:
- Chapter-1 Real Number
- Chapter-2 Polynomials
- Chapter-3 Pair of Linear Equations in Two Variables
- Chapter-4 Quadratic Equations
- Chapter-5 Arithmetic Progressions
- Chapter-6 Coordinate Geometry
- Chapter-7 Triangles
- Chapter-8 Circles
- Chapter-9 Introduction to Trigonometry
- Chapter-10 Trigonometric Identities
- Chapter-11 Heights and Distances
- Chapter-12 Areas Related to Circles
- Chapter-13 Surface Areas and Volumes
- Chapter-14 Statistics
- Chapter-15 Probability
2.0CBSE Class 10 Maths Books
NCERT CBSE Class 10 maths textbooks play an important role in students' learning processes. They add clarity and depth to concepts in the subject. For the 10th standard of CBSE, the only support a student looks forward to is NCERT books, known for their clear explanations, solved examples, and alignment with the syllabus. These books offer a systematic understanding of concepts, making them a key part of CBSE examinations.
NCERT Books for Class 10
The main source that explains each topic in detail and adheres to the CBSE curriculum is the NCERT Maths Textbook for class 10.
Additional Reference Books for Practice
- RD Sharma
- RS Aggarwal
These additional reference books are excellent for practising problems and reinforcing concepts covered in the NCERT textbooks.
3.0Class 10 Maths Chapter-wise Weightage
4.0Class 10 Mock Test
Mock Tests play a very important role in students' academic journeys. Practising regular mock tests can help students learn more about exam patterns, question types, and time management strategies. Mock Tests help students identify their areas of improvement and provide the platform to enhance their problem-solving skills and score better in the exam. Additionally, mock tests reduce exam anxiety by familiarising students with the exam format.
5.0Class 10 Math Preparation Tips
- Familiarize with the examination pattern: The students are advised to go through it once at the commencement of the session. This will help them understand the type of questions, the number of questions, and the marks allotted for each question.
- Know the Exam syllabus: Students should know the syllabus at the start of the session. This will help them to make a proper plan and strategy regarding their studies. They must also learn about the chapters and topics that need to be completed in the entire session. Try to cover and complete the syllabus by the end of December so you will have enough time to revise.
- Remember the formulae/theorems: Make another notebook where all the formulae for each chapter and topic are written. So, memorize the formulae and theorems, but most importantly, remember the derivation and application of the same. This will save you much time in the examination while solving any question.
- Plan out a study schedule: Students are recommended to make a study schedule to help them score good marks in the exam. The study timetable keeps one on the right path and maintains studies at a constant pace.
- Solve exercise problems from the NCERT book: NCERT is the key to scoring good marks in the CBSE Class 10 board Exams. Whatever is taught in the class, try to solve all cbse class 10 maths important questions from the NCERT.
- Solve Previous Year Question Papers: Practice through previous year maths question papers will make the students aware of the exam pattern and type of questions. Scoring full marks will seem easy and realistic if students solve the previous year's question papers.
- Practice SETs CBSE Maths Sample Question Papers: CBSE class 10 Maths sample paper with solutions clarify the structure and type of questions in the CBSE Maths board exam. Students are advised to solve the sample paper and view its solution, which they can use as a guide for exam preparation.
CBSE Class 10 2025 Mathematics exam can be easily conquered if one prepares sincerely and with the right approach. Use the resources available to you; practice consistently; believe in yourself. Do the required hard work, and success will definitely follow.

CBSE Class 8
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) administers the class 8 exam, a prerequisite for all NCERT-based schools to be affiliated with CBSE. The students need to stay in touch with the school authorities regularly so they get to know the dates of examination or else they can also see the official website.
1.0CBSE Class 8 Subjects
CBSE Class 8 include five major subjects which form the foundation for higher studies and provide a comprehensive understanding of various concepts:
- English
- Hindi
- Math
- Science
- Social Science
It is up to the schools to add Sanskrit, Computer Science, Art, G.K. and Physical Education as extra subjects to the curriculum.
Class 8 is a significant part of a student's academic journey through higher studies. The class 8 printed study material (available at ALLEN estore) at this stage is designed to assist students in developing analytical thinking, problem-solving, and logical reasoning skills. These abilities, in turn, lead to academic success and personal development.
2.0CBSE Class 8 Syllabus
The CBSE Board carefully designs the syllabus for Class 8, which focuses on laying a solid foundation for higher classes. Students must check the syllabus to ensure they study effectively for the exam and achieve good scores.
There are some changes in the New Education Policy (NEP) which every student in class 8 must ensure:
- Students from any school across India are now required to study three languages. One language can be a foreign language, and two can be native Indian languages.
- Every student has to learn coding in school.
Also Check: Subject-wise syllabus for CBSE Class 8
3.0CBSE Class 8 Exam Pattern
The class 8 exam pattern is designed carefully by the CBSE board, evaluating a student’s knowledge of the study material with different tests and exams conducted throughout the academic year. Here is the summary of the CBSE Class 6 Exam Pattern:
Type of Exams
Term 1: The assessment for the first half of the session consists of a half-yearly exam and a periodic test totalling 100 marks.
- Periodic Test 1: This 20-mark test is typically held after a few months into the school year.
- Half-Yearly Exam: This 80-mark exam covers the first half of the academic year's syllabus and is conducted at the end of the first term.
Term 2: Similar to the Term 1 exam, the total assessment for the second half of the session is also 100 marks.
- Periodic Test 2: This 20-mark test is conducted midway through the second term.
- Annual Exam: The annual exams, conducted at the end of the academic year, cover the entire syllabus, including the second term and sometimes a few topics from the first term.
Exam Format
- Objective-type questions: These include multiple-choice questions (MCQs), fill-in-the-blanks, true/false, match the following, etc.
- Short Answer Questions: These are typically 2-4 marks each, requiring brief explanations or problem-solving.
- Long Answer Questions: These are usually 5-8 marks each and require detailed explanations, reasoning, or problem-solving.
- Weightage: The distribution of marks among different questions varies, with long-answer questions generally carrying more weight.
Marking Scheme
- Total Marks: Each subject typically has 80-100 marks.
- Internal Assessment: In some schools, internal assessments (such as projects, oral tests, or class activities) account for 10-20% of the final grade.
- Final Exam Marks: Depending on the school's policy, the written paper might carry 80-90% of the total marks for the annual exam.
Internal Assessment
Teachers may include internal assessments as part of the overall evaluation. These may include:
- Project Work: Assignments and projects on various subjects.
- Class Participation: Active participation in classroom activities and discussions.
- Oral Tests: Oral tests to assess reading, speaking, and listening abilities.
- Notebook Submission: Regularity and neatness of notebook submissions.
Suggested Weightage
Here is the weightage distribution for the assessments at various CBSE Class 6 exam stages.
- Periodic Tests: 10% each (Term 1 and Term 2)
- Notebook Submission: 5%
- Subject Enrichment Activities: 5%
- Half-Yearly Exam: 40%
- Annual Exam: 40%
4.0CBSE Class 8 Study Materials
Class 8 study materials are designed with the vast NCERT syllabus in mind, ensuring coverage of all the subjects and topics so students can grasp concepts quickly and prepare for their exams.
- NCERT Textbooks:
NCERT textbooks are the most crucial study materials since they cover the entire curriculum and set the foundation for Class 8.
English - HoneyDew; It So Happened
Hindi - वसंत भाग 3, दूर्वा भाग 3
Math - Mathematics NCERT Textbook
Science - Science NCERT Textbook
Social Science - NCERT Textbooks of Civics, History & Geography
- Additional Books:
Mathematics: RD Sharma and RS Aggarwal for additional practice problems.
Science: Use the Lakhmir Singh and Manjit Kaur books for detailed explanations and practice questions, as well as S. Chand for various questions to practise.
Social Science: Together With Social Science and All in One Social Science by Arihant.
CBSE Class 8 Preparation and Strategy
- Understand the Syllabus: The first step in your academic journey is to thoroughly understand the entire school syllabus of the CBSE curriculum in all subjects. This will help you identify areas that are vital and decide the way you will spend your study time.
- Create a Study Schedule: Create a timetable with dedicated study time for each subject. Maintain a balance across all subjects, devoting more time to areas where you can improve.
- Regular Revision: As part of the weekly schedule, set aside some time to go over what you have learned. This action helps strengthen your memory, allowing you to retain the concepts for a longer time. For summarising and quick revision, the note creation or use of a flashcard.
- Practise with PYQs: Solve previous years' papers of standard question papers to enhance and review your skills. Furthermore, familiarise yourself with the examination pattern and the types of questions.
- Take Care of Your Health: Take regular breaks from your study sessions to relax and rejuvenate. This will prevent fatigue and maintain your motivation.

CBSE Class 10 Admit Card 2026 | Download Admit Card
The CBSE Class 10 Admit Card 2026 has finally been released by the Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) for students appearing in the 2026 board examinations. With board exams just weeks away, this crucial document confirms a student’s eligibility to appear in the exam and contains key information including exam dates, centre details, roll number, and candidate credentials. Students must ensure they have their admit card in original form on all exam days to avoid entry refusal at the examination hall.
Latest Update – Admit Cards Officially Released
- CBSE has released the 2026 Class 10 admit cards for board exams on the official Pariksha Sangam portal and the CBSE website.
- Board exams starts on February 17, 2026 and strict entry rules apply, including carrying the original admit card.
- Private candidates can directly download their hall tickets online, while regular students must collect them through their schools.
This release comes as part of the board’s preparations ahead of the Class 10 board exams. Notably, no student will be permitted to enter the exam centre without a valid admit card, making it one of the most important documents for any board aspirant.
1.0CBSE Class 10 Admit Card: Important dates
Here are the tentative timelines related to the CBSE Class 10 Admit Card:
2.0How to Download CBSE Class 10 Admit Card
For Regular Students (Through School Login)
Regular students enrolled in CBSE-affiliated schools cannot download their admit cards directly from the CBSE website. Instead, their respective school administrations are responsible for downloading and distributing them.
Here’s how schools obtain the admit cards:
- Visit the official CBSE website [cbse.gov.in]
- Go to the “Pariksha Sangam” portal available on the homepage.
- Click on the “School Login” option.
- Enter the User ID, Password, and Security Code provided by the board.
- Navigate to the “Admit Card/Examination Materials” section.
- Select the option for “CBSE Class 10 Admit Card 2026”.
- Download the admit cards for all registered students.
- Print them out and stamp/sign each admit card before distributing it to students.
Important Note: Every student must collect their admit card from their school office. It should bear the official school stamp and principal’s signature, without which it will be considered invalid on exam day.
For Private Candidates (Direct Download Method)
Private or independent candidates appearing for the CBSE Class 10 Board Exams 2026 can download their admit card online directly through the CBSE portal.
Follow these steps carefully:
- Visit the Official CBSE Website [cbse.gov.in]
- Select “Private Candidate Admit Card 2026.”
- Enter Login Credentials:
You can log in using any one of the following:- Application Number
- Previous Roll Number & Year
- Candidate Name & Mother’s Name
- Verify Captcha & Submit:
Enter the security code and click on Submit. - View & Download Admit Card:
Your CBSE Class 10 Admit Card 2026 will appear on the screen. Review all details carefully. - Print the Admit Card:
Click on ‘Print’ and take multiple copies. Carry the original printout to every exam.
3.0Details Mentioned in the CBSE Class 10 Admit Card 2026
The following details will be mentioned in the admit card of the students for the CBSE Class 10 Exams
- Student Profile: Full Name, Roll Number, Mother’s Name, Father’s Name, and Date of Birth.
- Physical Identification: Candidate’s Photograph and scanned Signature.
- Centre Information: Exam Centre Name, Centre Code, and School Code.
- Subject Schedule: A list of subjects (e.g., Physics, Accountancy, History) with their specific Subject Codes and Exam Dates.
- Authorisations: Signature of the Controller of Examinations and a designated space for the School Principal’s signature.
4.0Important Instructions for Students Regadring Admit Card for Class 10 Exams
- Ensure to carry the printed admit card to the exam centre on all exam days.
- The admit card must be signed and stamped by the principal for it to be allowed into the examination hall.
- Reach the examination centre at least 30 minutes before the reporting time to avoid last-minute hurry and confusion.
- Make sure to verify all the information in the admit card. Notify the school authorities in case of any discrepancy.
- Do not carry prohibited items such as mobile phones, smartwatches, or written material into the examination hall.
- Read all the instructions printed on the admit card strictly and follow them thoroughly.
- Keep the admit card safe even after exams, as it may be required during result checking or admissions.
- Do not laminate the admit card until all the exams are finished.

CBSE - Central Board of Secondary Education
CBSE is a national-level board of education in India, responsible for public and private school education, and is managed by the Government of India. CBSE-affiliated schools, which can be both government-run and privately managed, follow the National Council of Educational Research and Training (NCERT) curriculum, designed to be student-friendly and focus on holistic development.
CBSE conducts major examinations, including the All India Secondary School Examination (AISSE) for Class 10 and the All India Senior School Certificate Examination (AISSCE) for Class 12. Instruction in CBSE schools is primarily offered in English or Hindi.
The Full Form of CBSE is the Central Board of Secondary Education. CBSE aims to provide top-quality education that helps students grow intellectually, socially, and culturally. The goal is to nurture students into becoming future global leaders. The board has a widespread presence with over 24,000 affiliated schools across India and around 240 schools in 26 countries worldwide, covering various categories such as government schools, private independent schools, and international schools.
1.0CBSE Exam Dates
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) usually conducts its major examinations for Class 10 and Class 12 in February and March. The detailed schedule for these exams is typically released in December or January on its official website - cbse.gov.in, giving students ample time to prepare and plan their study schedules.
Admit cards for the exams are generally issued a few weeks before the exams commence. Schools distribute them to the students, or they can be downloaded from the CBSE portal.
To aid in preparation, CBSE's website provides various resources, such as sample papers, previous year question papers, and marking schemes, helping students prepare effectively.
2.0CBSE Exam Syllabus
For students in Classes 6 through 12, the Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) offers a thorough and organized curriculum. The curriculum is created to support an all-encompassing education and guarantee that social, emotional, and cognitive skills are developed in a balanced manner.
To guarantee that students receive a current and relevant education, the CBSE syllabus is updated on a regular basis to reflect new requirements and trends in education.
3.0CBSE Exam Pattern 2025-26
Classes 1 to 8
The weightage of formative and summative assessments varies across classes and schools, with an emphasis on overall development and skill acquisition rather than strict examinations. This approach ensures that students are evaluated on a broad range of abilities, promoting a more holistic educational experience.
Classes 9 and 10
Students will be evaluated through both Formative and Summative Assessments. Summative Assessments will account for 60% of the overall grade, while the remaining 40% will come from Formative Assessments. The Formative Assessments will focus on evaluating students' calculations and various abilities.
Classes 11 and 12
CBSE has made major changes to the exam pattern for Classes 11 and 12 starting from the 2024-25 school year. The new pattern focuses on moving away from rote learning to ensure students better understand concepts and how to apply them in real life.
Check CBSE Class 12 exam pattern to know the detailed marking scheme and exam pattern for Class 12.
Key Changes in the CBSE Exam Pattern:
4.0CBSE Study Materials
CBSE study material is essential for students as it aligns with the curriculum, ensuring thorough coverage of all topics. It offers structured learning, helping students build a solid foundation and understand concepts step-by-step. By supporting self-study and accommodating diverse learning styles with visual aids and real-life examples, CBSE materials prepare students for school exams, competitive exams, and higher education. Using precise and organized study materials ensures comprehensive coverage, clear understanding, and effective practice, leading to improved scores and academic success.
CBSE provides a variety of study material on their official website:
- Previous Years' Question Papers
One of the best ways to learn time management is by solving Previous Year's Question Papers. They will help you understand the exam pattern and marking scheme. With regular practice of sample papers, you can enhance your performance in the exam by working on your weak areas and starting to improve them. According to the latest updates, CBSE has not released question papers for the upcoming exam, but you can find CBSE past year papers for classes 10 and 12 at ALLEN.
- CBSE Board Exam Sample Papers
CBSE releases sample papers so that students of classes 10 and 12 are aware of the exam pattern and marking. To make them more confident in solving the actual paper in the D-day. You can easily access the CBSE Class 10 and 12 sample papers on the official website.

IIT JEE Physics
Physics is one of the core subjects of IIT JEE. It is that part of science which deals with the study of basic principles governing the behaviour of matter and energy. It deals with "how" and "why" behind motion, interaction, and transformation of everything—from subatomic particles to phenomena in the cosmos.
Physics for IIT JEE mainly deals with some core concepts, such as force, energy, mass, and charge, and how these interact in various contexts. You have to master these things, as they form the base through which most of the questions you would meet while taking the exam are asked.
Study of Physics for JEE will not only help in grasping the basic laws of the universe but will also hone problem-solving skills, which are critical for success in the exam. The in-depth understanding of the subject will greatly help in bringing high marks and top rank in the JEE.
1.0IIT JEE Physics Study Material
Physics has an aim to come out with the universal laws that would govern the physical world in the form of mathematical equations and models. These would describe natural phenomena and would be tested for the validation of theories and discovery of new phenomena through experiments.
One should be able to master the models given in JEE Physics and use them to predict the behavior of physical systems. Since many new technologies are based on central physics principles, it is important not only for the exam but also from the point of view of future developments.
IIT JEE Physics Important Topics - JEE Main and Advanced
2.0Branches of Physics
Physics is a diverse and multifaceted field, encompassing various branches that explore different aspects of the physical world. Each branch focuses on specific phenomena and applications. Physics can be classified into various branches based on the phenomena, methods and applications. Some of the branches are categorized as:
A. Classical Mechanics: It is a branch of physics dedicated to examining the movement of objects and the forces that affect them. It provides a framework for understanding various physical phenomena, from everyday experiences to complex mechanical systems. Classical mechanics theory is primarily based on fundamental principles and equations that describe how objects move and interact with forces.
The Fundamental Principles of Classical Mechanics are:
- Newton’s Laws of Motion
- Law of Conservation
- Kinematics
- Dynamics
- Equations of Motion
- Work and Energy
- Rotational Dynamics
- Simple Harmonic Motion
B. Electromagnetism: This is a branch of physics that investigates how electric and magnetic fields influence each other and their effects on charged particles. It is a fundamental aspect of many technologies and natural phenomena.
The Fundamental Principles of Electromagnetism are:
- Electric Fields
- Magnetic Fields
- Electromagnetic Forces
- Maxwell’s Equations
- Electric Circuits
- Electromagnetic Induction
C. Thermodynamics: This branch of physics examines the connections between heat, work, and energy, and explores how these relationships influence the properties of matter. It provides fundamental principles that govern the behaviour of energy and matter in various systems.
The Fundamental Principles of Thermodynamics are:
- System and Surroundings
- State Variables
- Laws of Thermodynamics
- Processes and Cycles
D. Optics: It is the branch of physics that investigates the behavior and properties of light, focusing on its interactions with matter and the development of instruments for its use and detection. Optics encompasses the study of visible light as well as other forms of electromagnetic radiation
The Fundamental Principles of Optics are:
- Nature of Light
- Geometric Optics
- Optical Instruments
- Wave Optics
3.0Physics in Everyday Life
Physics is essential for several reasons, deeply influencing both our understanding of the natural world and our technological advancements. Some of the important aspect of Physics are:
- Technology and Electronics
- Communication
- Home Appliances
- Safety
- Transportation
4.0The Scope of Physics
Physics in JEE covers a very broad spectrum: from the most fundamental laws of the universe to instant applications in technology. On the other hand, Physics also accounts for some crucial processes, such as climatic change, seismic activity, magnetic fields, or anything else which may be part of the questions asked. Other parts include the actual working behind the functioning of common gadgets like smartphones, household appliances, and computers.
Physics is a very vast and broad subject and continues to grow. It is one of the basic sciences furnishing methods and concepts that enrich not just our current understanding of the universe but also empower you with knowledge to solve real-world problems and succeed in the JEE.

Karnataka School Examination and Assessment Board (KSEAB) – Overview, Exams, Syllabus & Resources
The Karnataka School Examination and Assessment Board (KSEAB) is the main state‐board that manages schooling for students in Karnataka, especially for Class 10 (SSLC) and Class 12 (2nd PUC).
If you are a student in Class 6-10, this page will help you understand what the board does, how your exams will be, what the syllabus is, and how you can use useful resources to prepare well.
1.0Quick Facts about the KSEAB
- Full Name: Karnataka School Examination and Assessment Board (KSEAB)
- Established Year: 1966
- Headquarters: Bengaluru (Malleshwaram area)
- Official Website: kseab.karnataka.gov.in (and related portals)
- Key Exams: SSLC (Class 10) & 2nd PUC (Class 12)
2.0History & Background of KSEAB
The board was set up in 1966 (as the then Secondary Education Examination Board) to bring uniformity in secondary education in Karnataka.
Over time, tasks such as designing syllabus, conducting board exams, and recognising schools have been taken over by the board. It covers thousands of schools across Karnataka and plays a key role in the academic journey of students from Class 6 to Class 12.
3.0Latest Updates / Notifications
- The board has approved pass marks of 33% (down from earlier 35%) for SSLC and 2nd PUC exams from the 2025-26 academic year.
- Sample question papers, topper answer sheets and blueprint details are being made available to students online to aid preparation.
4.0Role & Functions of the KSEAB Board
- Prescribing the syllabus and curriculum for secondary (Class 10) and higher secondary (Class 12) classes.
- Approving and publishing textbooks and study materials for affiliated schools.
- Conducting the board examinations for SSLC (Class 10) and PUC (Class 12) and declaring results.
- Recognising and affiliating schools under its jurisdiction in Karnataka.
- Ensuring transparency, timely evaluation and academic standards in schools.
5.0Major Examinations Conducted by KSEAB
The KSEAB primarily conducts 3 exams namely SSLC, PUC exams and Supplemenatry exams. The brief of the same is given below.
- SSLC (Secondary School Leaving Certificate) - Class 10: These SSLC exams are conducted thrice in an academic year namely Mid Term Exam, SSLC Exam 1 and SSLC Exam 2.
- 2nd PUC (Pre-University Course) - Class 12: Theseare conducted once a year and covers streams like Science, Commerce, Arts.
- Supplementary/Improvement Exams: These exams are for students who don’t pass in the main exam conducted in the regular schedule. However, the KSEAB has now removed the concept of supplementary exams which has now been replaced with 3 exams conducted through out the academic year.
6.0KSEAB Syllabus & Textbooks
The board publishes syllabus for Classes 6–12 so that students and schools know what topics will be covered.
Textbooks approved by the board are available in different mediums (Kannada, English, etc.) depending on the school.
For example, in Class 10 you’ll study First Language, Second Language, Mathematics, Science, Social Science. Schools provide these textbooks based on your medium of instruction.
7.0Exam Pattern & Marking Scheme
The exams for until class 9 is conducted by the schools affiliated with the board as per the prescribed syllabus by the KSEAB. Class 10 students will have 3 annual exams conducted in the months September, March/April and May. The details of the same is given below:
8.0Eligibility & Application Process
- Regular students: If you are studying in a school affiliated to KSEAB, your school will register you for the SSLC or PUC exams.
- Private / Ex‐school candidates: If you are not in a regular school or wish to appear as a private student, you need to apply through the board’s process (form, fee, deadlines) – check the official portal.
- Make sure your details (name, roll number, school, medium) are correct, pay required fees and submit the form on time.
9.0Resources for Students
- Previous Year Question Papers: Practice earlier years’ papers to understand question style and improve speed.
- Model Papers / Sample Papers: Designed as per board pattern — good for timed practice.
- Answer Keys / Solutions: To check your work, learn from mistakes and improve.
- Study Tips / Preparation Strategy:
- Create a study timetable covering all subjects.
- Understand topics instead of memorising.
- Solve full‐length papers under time limit.
- Revise topics regularly, especially those you find tough.
- Maintain good health and rest well during exam season.

JEE Main 2026
Latest Update: JEE Main 2026 Session 1 (January Session) Results OUT. NTA has declared JEE Main 2026 Session 1 Scores for Paper 2 (B.Arch and B.Planing)
JEE Main is a Nationwide Computer-Based Engineering Exam conducted by the National Testing Agency (NTA) and is a first step to securing admission in undergraduate courses (B.E./B.Tech/B.Planning/B.Arch) at some of the best engineering colleges, including NITs, IIITs, CFTIs, and other institutions/Universities participating by state governments in India.
The exam is generally conducted twice a year, in January and April. Every year, more than 10 lakh students appear for the JEE Main exam, out of which only 2.5 lakh successfully qualify and then appear for JEE Advanced.
The Joint Entrance Examination, JEE (Main) comprises two papers:
- Paper 1: For engineering programs (B.E./B.Tech.) at NITs, IIITs, and CFTIs, and as a prerequisite for JEE (Advanced) for IIT admissions.
- Paper-2A & Paper-2B: For B. Arch and B. Planning courses.
Students can also benefit from the ALLEN App when preparing for competitive exams such as JEE. Students can download the ALLEN App to make their learning more interactive, immersive, and engaging. Click here to understand the advantages of the ALLEN app.
Get complete details about JEE Main 2026, including expected exam schedule, application process, eligibility and syllabus etc.
1.0JEE Main 2026 Important Dates
Here are the important events and their respective tentative dates for the JEE Main 2026 exam:

3.0JEE Main Overview
Organizer – Since 2019, National Testing Agency (NTA)
Purpose –
- To get admission to Undergraduate Engineering Programs (B.E./B.Tech.) at NITs, IIITs, other CFTIs, and Institutions/Universities funded/recognized by participating State Governments.
- JEE (Main) is an eligibility test for JEE (Advanced).
Frequency – Twice in a year in January and April (Session-1 & Session-2).
This will give the candidates two opportunities to improve their examination scores if they are not able to give their best in one attempt.
Mode of Exam –
- Paper 1 (B.E. /B. Tech.): Online mode
- Paper 2A (B. Arch): Mathematics and Aptitude Test – Online mode, Drawing Test - Pen and paper mode.
- Paper 2B (B. Planning): Online mode
Exam Duration –
Marks –
Exam Medium –
Test Centres –
JEE Main Exam Pattern 2026 & Marking Scheme
Understanding the JEE Main exam pattern is essential for effective preparation. It gives students a clear idea of the marking scheme, question distribution, sectional weightage, and exam duration.
JEE Main Selection Process
The National Testing Agency (NTA) will merge the JEE Main 2026 scores for each candidate in B.E./B. Tech for Session 1 and Session 2 to compile the results and prepare the overall Merit List/Ranking. The best of the two NTA Scores (Total) for candidates who appeared in both sessions will be considered for further processing.
The All-India Rank shall be compiled and declared after the conduct of Session 2 of the JEE (Main) examination.
Cut-off Scores for the Last 6 years
4.0JEE Main Eligibility Criteria
Nationality Criteria
The candidate must be an Indian Citizen. Foreign Nationals, NRIs, PIOs, and OCIs are also eligible to apply.
Qualification Criteria
Candidates who have successfully passed any of the listed examinations are eligible to appear in JEE (Main).
List of Qualifying Examinations (QE)
- The final examination of the 10+2 system is conducted by any recognized Central/ State Board, such as the Central Board of Secondary Education, New Delhi, the Council for the Indian School Certificate Examinations, New Delhi, etc.
- Intermediate or two-year Pre-University examination conducted by a recognized Board/ University.
- Final examination of the two-year course of the Joint Services Wing of the National Defence Academy.
- Senior Secondary School Examination conducted by the National Institute of Open Schooling with a minimum of five subjects.
- The Association of Indian Universities (AIU) recognizes any public school/ Board/ University examination in India or any foreign country as equivalent to the 10+2 system.
- Higher Secondary Certificate Vocational Examination.
- A Diploma recognized by AICTE or a State board of technical education of at least 3 years duration.
- General Certificate Education (GCE) examination (London/Cambridge/Sri Lanka) at the Advanced (A) level.
- High School Certificate Examination of the Cambridge University or International Baccalaureate Diploma of the International Baccalaureate Office, Geneva.
- Candidates who have completed the Class 12 (or equivalent) examination outside India or from a Board not specified above should produce a certificate from the Association of Indian Universities (AIU) to the effect that the examination they have passed is equivalent to the Class 12 Examination.
- If the Class 12 Examination is not a public examination, the candidate must have passed at least one public (Board or pre-university) examination earlier.
5.0Subject Criteria in the Qualifying Exam
Age Criteria
There is no age limit. Eligible candidates include those who passed the class 12th examination in 2024, 2025 or appearing in 2026.
State of Eligibility
The State Code of Eligibility in JEE Main is based on the state where a candidate completed their Class XII (or equivalent) exam. It's not related to their place of birth or residency.
Example - if a candidate appears for the Class 12 (or equivalent) qualifying examination from an Institution situated in New Delhi and is a resident of Noida, Uttar Pradesh, then the candidate’s State code of eligibility will be that of Delhi and not that of Uttar Pradesh.
6.0JEE Main Syllabus
Knowing the syllabus of the JEE Main exam helps students focus on the most important topics throughout the preparation journey. The JEE Main syllabus 2026 encompasses chapters and topics of Physics, Chemistry and Mathematics from NCERT Class 11 and Class 12 textbooks.
SYLLABUS for Paper-1 (B.E./B.Tech.)
Physics – Achieving success in the JEE Main exam heavily relies on the importance of the physics subject. To excel, prioritize crucial topics and diligently practice questions from both theoretical and practical applications.
Chemistry – The JEE Main Chemistry syllabus mainly emphasizes theoretical concepts, making this subject more manageable than Physics and Mathematics. It is categorized into three Main sections: Physical Chemistry, Inorganic Chemistry, and Organic Chemistry.
Mathematics – Every concept in the JEE Main Maths syllabus is important, as questions can be asked from any part of it. Accuracy and Speed are key when solving JEE Main Maths questions, and consistent practice is the most effective way to enhance both.

13.0JEE Main Counselling Procedure
JoSAA: Joint Seat Allocation Authority
- The Ministry of Education (formerly known as the Ministry of Human Resources Development, MHRD) has established the Joint Seat Allocation Authority (JoSAA) to oversee and regulate the joint seat allocation process for admission to a total of 119 institutions.
- These institutions encompass various categories, including 23 prestigious IITs, 31 NITs, IIEST Shibpur, 26 IIITs, and 38 Other Technical Institutes that receive full or partial funding from either the Central or State Government (Other-GFTIs).
Eligibility for JoSAA Counselling
Who Can Apply for JoSAA Counselling?
- 12th Class Completion: You must have successfully cleared your 12th-grade examinations.
- JEE Main Qualification: Qualification in the JEE Main exam is essential.
- IITs with JEE Advanced: If you've cleared JEE Advanced, you can apply for IITs.
- Others (NITs, IIITs, GFTIs): If you've met JEE Main requirements, you can apply for NITs, IIITs, and GFTIs.
JoSAA 2025 Counselling Process
The JoSAA counselling process for 2025 comprises several steps, ensuring a structured and transparent admission journey.
Step 1: Registration
Begin by registering on the official JoSAA portal using your JEE Main 2025 credentials.
Step 2: Choice Filling for Courses and Institutes
Select your preferred branches and institutions based on your qualifications and preferences.
Step 3: Choice Locking
Lock in your selected choices and ensure you have a printout for reference.
Step 4: Seat Allocation
Seats are allocated based on your rank, program and institute choices, and seat availability.
Step 5: Document Verification
Document verification is conducted online, and it's a crucial step before proceeding further.
Step 6: Reporting at the Institute
Finally, report to the allotted institute to complete your admission by submitting the remaining fee and fulfilling other formalities.
Participating Colleges List
14.0JEE Main Exam History
- The IIT-JEE (Indian Institutes of Technology Joint Entrance Examination) was first conducted in 1961. It was an entrance examination in India for admission to various undergraduate programs in engineering and technology at the prestigious Indian Institutes of Technology (IITs) and other participating institutions.
- In 2002, an additional exam called the AIEEE was introduced for admissions to many institutions of national importance other than the IITs.
- In 2013, the AIEEE was renamed JEE (Main), and IIT-JEE was renamed JEE (Advanced); the JEE (Main) became the screening exam for JEE (Advanced).
What is the NTA?
The JEE Main exam is administered and conducted by the authority known as the National Testing Agency (NTA). NTA is the agency responsible for conducting the JEE Main every year. NTA is also responsible for deciding the eligibility criteria, setting question papers, releasing admit cards, declaring results, and other related activities. For preparation assistance, NTA also releases mock tests and lectures for subjects including Mathematics, Physics, Biology, and Chemistry, along with mock test papers for all entrance exams (accessible to those who register for the particular exam).
It has recently introduced changes that address the inclusion of candidates with disabilities. Such changes can also be observed in the JEE Main eligibility criteria 2024 designed by NTA.

7.0JEE Advanced 2026 Mock Test: Navigate Through JEE Questions & Sections
Here is what you need to know about sections in the JEE Advanced Mock test. Learn how to navigate through the exam sections to avoid any issues during the actual.
- The JEE Advanced 2026 Mock Test will contain three sections similar to the actual exam pattern: Math, Chemistry, and Physics.
- As per the JEE Advanced exam pattern, every section will contain MCQ-type questions, multiple correct answers, matching questions, and numerical questions.
- The candidate can easily navigate the questions and sections while attempting the mock test. To do so:
- Candidates will find a question palette (list) on the right side of their computer screen. They can click on a particular question to move on to that question.
- Note that moving on to the next question will not save the response you have given for the current question. First, save the response/answer, and then go to a different question, the candidate must tap the button “Save and Next” for the same.
- If the candidate feels unsure about a particular answer and might have to review it later, they may mark that question by tapping on the button “Mark for Review & Next.” This will remind them later to come back to that question. This can be done for every question, whether you have answered it or not.
- On top of the page, candidates can view and navigate the different parts and sections at any time during the JEE 2026 exam.
- A section summary is also available above the question listing for every section, which candidates can refer to anytime during the test.
8.0JEE Advanced 2026 Mock Test: Points to Remember
Candidates must note the following important instructions before attempting the mock test:
- You will be given a total of three hours for the JEE mock test, including the three different sections. Once 180 minutes are completed, the test will automatically get submitted.
- The exam timer will begin when you start the mock test by clicking on ‘I agree.’ Candidates can also see the remaining time at the top right corner of the computer screen anytime during the test.
- Candidates can choose the language in which they want to view the questions. They can change the language anytime during the test by clicking on the candidate's profile image and choosing the desired language.

NEET Exam Pattern 2026
The NEET exam pattern for 2026, which will be set by the National Testing Agency (NTA) is crucial for candidates preparing for the examination. As per the latest exam pattern, there will be 180 compulsory questions (45 questions each in Physics and Chemistry and 90 in Biology), which the candidates will attempt in 180 minutes (3 hours). Candidates are advised to prepare accordingly and regularly check the official NTA website neet.nta.nic.in for the latest updates.
1.0NEET-UG 2026 to be Conducted in Pen and Paper mode on a single day
The examination will be conducted in pen and paper mode across various centres in India and abroad.
2.0What is the Pattern of the NEET 2026 Exam?
Paper pattern for the NEET exam comprises three subjects: Physics, Chemistry, and Biology. There will be 180 compulsory questions (45 questions each in Physics and Chemistry and 90 in Biology), which the candidates will attempt in 180 minutes (3 hours).
NEET 2026 will be a one-tiered scoring system exam conducted by the National Testing Agency (NTA) for admission to medical and dental colleges, covering both private and public institutions. Prospective students seeking graduate and post-graduate medical and dentistry programs should familiarize themselves with the exam pattern for NEET to prepare effectively.
Also Read: NEET 2026 Syllabus
Paper Pattern for NEET 2026 - Highlights
3.0NEET (UG) 2026 Question Paper Overview
4.0NEET Exam Marking Scheme
Prospective NEET 2026 candidates will be able to estimate their exam scores by using the official answer key provided by NTA. To do this, it will be important to understand how NTA's scoring system works. Here's a simplified breakdown:
- Each question is worth 4 marks.
- Choosing the correct or most suitable answer: +4 marks.
- Marking an incorrect option: -1 mark.
- Leaving a question unanswered: 0 marks.
- When multiple options are correct, +4 marks are awarded to those who choose any of the correct options.
- If all options are correct, +4 marks are given to all who attempt the question.
- If none of the options is correct, a question is wrong, or it's dropped, all candidates receive +4 marks, regardless of whether they attempted the question.
5.0NEET (UG) Languages/Medium of Exam
NEET 2026 exam will be available in 13 different languages, depending on the exam center you pick.
Here are some important things to keep in mind about your language choice:
- Candidates can pick their question paper language from the following options: English, Hindi, Assamese, Bengali, Gujarati, Malayalam, Kannada, Marathi, Odia, Tamil, Telugu, Urdu, or Punjabi.
- The choice of language is made during the form-filling process and cannot be changed later.
- Those selecting English will get an English test booklet.
- Hindi language choosers will receive a bilingual test booklet in Hindi and English.
- Candidates opting for vernacular languages will receive a bilingual test booklet in their chosen language and English.
- If there's any uncertainty in the translation of a question, the English version will be regarded as the ultimate reference.
NEET 2026 Language Selection
6.0Important Points to Remember
Important Points to Remember while choosing the NEET 2026 Medium:
- Language once chosen for NEET 2026 cannot be changed later. The candidates can choose the medium of the question paper in their application form.
- Candidates with the preferred medium as English will be provided with the question paper in English medium ONLY.
- Candidates opting for any other medium will be provided with a bilingual question paper in their chosen language and English.
- In case of any doubt/error/ambiguity in any language, the English version of the question paper will be considered final and correct.

JEE Advanced Eligibility Criteria
Latest Update: IIT Roorkee has released the updated eligibility guidelines, confirming that there is no change in the number of attempts allowed for JEE Advanced 2026. Only two attempts at the exam—one in the year of completing 12th grade and the other in the following year will be allowed.
Students must meet the specific eligibility criteria set by the exam conducting authority (IIT) to appear for JEE Advanced 2026. Failure to meet the eligibility criteria will disqualify a candidate from taking the JEE Advanced exam. To avoid any discrepancies in the future, candidates are advised to carefully review the JEE Advanced eligibility criteria and thoroughly check the application form before submitting it.
The top JEE Main 2026 applicants will have the opportunity to register for JEE Advanced 2026. Staying updated with the dates is essential for a well-prepared and successful journey through JEE Advanced 2026.
1.0JEE Advanced Eligibility Criteria 2026: Overview
The requirements for admission into IITs through JEE Advanced include qualifying the JEE Main Exam, minimum marks in 12th, and more. Candidates will be straightaway disqualified if the JEE Advanced 2026 qualifying requirements are not met. The JEE Advanced eligibility criteria encompass various aspects, including age, the number of attempts, and qualifying exam marks. Below, you will find a detailed breakdown of the eligibility criteria for JEE Advanced Examination.
Criterion 1 - Qualifying Marks in JEE Main 2026
The primary and most essential eligibility criterion for the JEE Advanced 2026 exam is related to the qualifying marks and rank achieved based on a candidate's performance in the JEE Main 2026 exam (Paper - 1). Candidates must have successfully cleared JEE Main 2026 (Paper 1) and must be among the top 2,50,000 candidates who have cleared the JEE Main exam to qualify for JEE Adv 2026.
The division of categories is as follows:
- OPEN - 40.5%
- GEN-EWS - 10%
- OBC-NCL - 27%
- SC - 15%
- ST - 7.5%
- PwD - 5% horizontal reservation
Here the table below explains the order of selection for the top 2,50,000 candidates in different categories based on their performance in the JEE (Main) B.E./B.Tech. paper.
*Note - The total number of candidates may exceed 2,50,000 due to tied ranks or scores in any category.
Criterion 2 - Number of Attempts Allowed
Candidates are limited to a maximum of two attempts in two consecutive years for the JEE Advanced exam. No candidate would be allowed to sit for the JEE Advanced 2026 exam more than twice in two consecutive years. This practice has been consistent for several years and is expected to remain the same for the upcoming JEE Advanced 2026 exam.
Criterion 3 - Age Limit
The eligibility criteria for IIT JEE, which include age limits and age relaxation for certain categories, are based on the most current information available. Candidates born on or after 1st October 2001 are eligible for JEE Advanced 2026. Candidates in the PwD, ST, or SC categories are granted a 5-year relaxation in the age limit criteria, making those born on or after 1st October 1996 eligible for JEE Advanced 2026.
However, it's essential to remain updated with any changes in these criteria of age limit for IIT JEE Advanced 2026 that may occur after the official release of the notification for JEE Advanced 2026 by the conducting authority.
Criterion 4 - Previous Admissions at IITs
Candidates who have already been admitted to an IIT or ISM are not eligible to appear for JEE Advanced 2026. This includes candidates who were admitted to an IIT but subsequently expelled or rejected.
However, candidates who were first-time enrolled in a preparation programme at any of the IITs in 2025 may sit for the JEE Advanced exam in 2026. If a candidate got selected and given a seat in any IIT via the JoSAA 2025, however, was
(a) Not able to report at the “official reporting center” or online,
(b) Canceled before the last round of allocating seats, or
(c) Withdraw before the last round of allocating seats, then they are eligible to give the JEE Advanced 2026 exam.
Criterion 5 - Having Appeared in the Examination for Class 12 or Equivalent
- Candidates who have appeared for the class 10+2 examination in 2025 and 2026 with Physics, Chemistry, and Mathematics as compulsory subjects are eligible for the JEE Advanced 2026 Exam.
- Candidates who had appeared in the Class XII (or equivalent) examination for the first time in 2024 or earlier are NOT eligible to participate in JEE (Advanced) 2026, regardless of the combination of subjects they attempted or offered.
- However, candidates from that Board who took the Class XII (or equivalent) exam in 2024 are also eligible to take the JEE (Advanced) 2026 exam, as long as they fulfil all other eligibility requirements, if the examination board for that class announced the results for the academic year 2023–2024 on or after September 21, 2024. If the results for the academic year 2023–2024 were announced by the Class XII (or equivalent) Examination Board before September 21, 2024, and a candidate's results were withheld for any reason, that candidate will not be eligible to take the JEE (Advanced) 2026 exam.
To become JEE Advanced eligible, the candidate must also satisfy the requirements outlined in Criteria 1 through Criteria 5 in each of the mentioned situations.
Candidates who meet the above 5 criteria are eligible to complete the JEE Advanced 2026 application form. For those in search of a top JEE coaching institute, ALLEN is an excellent choice. At ALLEN, we provide students with well-organized study materials optimized to align with their study schedules. This comprehensive approach serves as a roadmap to successfully conquer the competitive JEE exams in a single attempt.
2.0Eligibility Criteria for Foreign Students or OCI/PIO Candidates
Foreign national candidates and OCI/PO (Overseas Citizen of India/Person of Indian Origin) candidates are not required to write JEE (Main) 2026. They can directly register for JEE Advanced 2026, as long as they meet the other specified eligibility criteria. This simplifies the process for their participation in the JEE Advanced examination.
ALLEN provides a detailed explanation of the JEE Advanced 2026 eligibility criteria for Foreign Students or OCI/PIO Candidates mentioned below:
Criterion A : JEE Advanced Age Limit
- Aspirants should be born on or after October 1, 1999.
- In countries where mandatory military service is required before/after Class XII (or equivalent) examination, candidates will receive a relaxation of the required number of years.
- Candidates must upload a certificate or testimonial issued by the competent authority to confirm this at the time of registration.
Criterion B : Number of Attempts
The JEE Advanced attempt limit is restricted to a maximum of two times in consecutive years.
Criterion C : Appearance in Class XII (or equivalent) examination
- Candidates must have taken the Class XII (or equivalent) examination for the first time in 2025 or 2026 with Physics, Chemistry, and Mathematics as compulsory subjects.
- Candidates who appeared for the Class XII (or equivalent) examination for the first time in 2024 or earlier are not eligible for JEE (Advanced) 2026, regardless of the subjects attempted.
- If the results for the academic year 2023-24 were declared by the examination board on or after September 23, 2023, candidates from that board who appeared for their Class XII (or equivalent) examination in 2024 are eligible for JEE (Advanced) 2026, provided they meet all other eligibility criteria.
Criterion D : Earlier admission at IITs
- Candidates who have been admitted to an IIT in the past, even if they did not continue in the program, are NOT eligible.
- Candidates whose admission to IITs was canceled after joining for any reason are also NOT eligible.
- However, candidates allocated a seat in an IIT through JoSAA 2025 but didn't report online or at a reporting center, withdrew before the last round of seat allotment, or had their seat canceled for any reason before the final round of seat allotment for IITs are eligible for JEE (Advanced) 2026.
For more information, Students can visit the official website of JEE Advanced 2026 jeeadv.ac.in/ to check the eligibility criteria.

  Title  : Up Board
  URL    : https://allen.in/up-board/
==============================================================================

Uttar Pradesh Madhyamik Shiksha Parishad (UPMSP)—Overview, Exams, Syllabus & Study Material
The Uttar Pradesh Madhyamik Shiksha Parishad (UPMSP), commonly known as the UP Board, is the state-government-administered examining authority that oversees school education and conducts the High School (Class 10) and Intermediate (Class 12) exams in Uttar Pradesh, India.
In this article, we will guide students through the main functions of the UP Board, explain how its key examinations are conducted, and provide an overview of the prescribed Class 10 and Class 12 syllabus, exam pattern etc. Students can also explore the best study materials to boost their preparation and achieve high exam scores.
1.0UPMSP: Board Overview
2.0Latest Updates/ Notifications from UPMSP (Tentative 2025-26 Cycle)
Students can stay updated with the latest announcements from UPMSP for the 2025–26 academic cycle here. We will cover important notifications, exam-related updates, and key information released by the board at the earliest. Students can check here regularly to stay informed about any changes or new guidelines.
3.0Major Examinations Conducted by the UPMSP
The UPMSP conducts major board examinations in Uttar Pradesh, including the High School (Class 10) and Intermediate (Class 12) exams. This section provides an overview of each exam:
4.0Syllabus & Textbooks prescribed by the UPMSP
The Board ensures that the syllabi for the High School and Intermediate levels are revised and updated periodically to align with the state’s educational needs.
- High School (Class 10): Core subjects typically include Hindi, English, Mathematics, Science, Social Science, and an optional subject such as drawing.
- Intermediate (Class 12): Students choose from various streams, like Science (PCM/PCB), Commerce, Arts, and Vocational subjects. The syllabus covers the required subjects for the selected stream.
- Textbooks: Textbooks are either published or approved by the UPMSP and are available in Hindi, English, and other regional mediums depending on the school's instructional language. Students are advised to refer to the official UP Board website for the latest syllabus PDFs and prescribed textbook lists.
5.0Exam Pattern & Duration
The board follows a structured pattern for its main examinations.
Note: The question paper pattern has increasingly incorporated objective-type questions and Multiple-Choice Questions (MCQs) in recent years. The use of barcoded answer booklets has also been introduced to ensure transparency and prevent malpractice.
6.0Eligibility & Application Process
- Regular Students: Students enrolled in an UPMSP-affiliated school are registered for the board examination by the school administration when they reach Class 10 or Class 12.
- Private/Ex-Students: Candidates not studying in a regular school may apply as private candidates following the specific guidelines, forms, fees, and deadlines published on the official UPMSP website.
- Verification: It is crucial for all students to verify that their personal details (name, date of birth, subjects, etc.) on the registration form and admit card are absolutely correct.
7.0Study Resources for Students by the UPMSP
The UPMSP provides essential study materials and resources to help students prepare for their board examinations:
- Previous Year Question Papers: Solving past papers is vital for understanding the question format, distribution of marks, and identifying frequently asked topics.
- Model Papers/Sample Papers: The board often releases official Model Papers which closely follow the expected pattern of the current year's exam.
- Answer Keys & Solutions: These help students check their practice performance and understand the expected method of answering questions.
8.0UP Board (UPMSP) History and Background
The UP Board is the first education board established in India. It was set up in 1921 by an act of the United Provinces Legislative Council in Prayagraj.
Before its formation, the University of Prayagraj served as the examining body for these levels. The UPMSP was established to ensure uniformity and standardisation in secondary and senior secondary education across the state. Notably, the board adopted the 10+2 examination system from its inception. Today, the UPMSP is a massive organisation that manages the academic future of approximately 50–70 lakh students annually.
Role of the UPMSP
The UPMSP is the supreme authority for secondary education in the state and performs a wide range of academic and administrative duties:
- Curriculum & Syllabus: Prescribing the curriculum, course of instruction, and detailed syllabus for High School (Class 10) and Intermediate (Class 12) levels.
- Examinations: Conducting the main annual board examinations for Class 10 (High School) and Class 12 (Intermediate).
- Affiliation & Recognition: Granting recognition and affiliation to schools operating under its jurisdiction in Uttar Pradesh.
- Textbooks: Publishing or approving textbooks and other learning materials for affiliated schools.
- Results & Records: Evaluating answer sheets, declaring the results, and maintaining records of student performance.
- Supplementary Exams: Conducting supplementary or improvement examinations for students who are unable to pass the main board exams.
9.0Study Tips / Preparation Strategy
- Structured Study Schedule: Create a realistic daily study timetable that allocates sufficient time for all subjects.
- Focus on Concepts: Prioritise understanding fundamental concepts over rote memorisation.
- Practice with Timers: Attempt full-length model tests under timed conditions to improve speed and time management.
- Review and Revise: Pay extra attention to your weaker areas, but ensure all subjects are continuously revised.
- Well-being: Maintain a healthy routine with good sleep, nutrition, and rest, especially in the run-up to the exams.

ICSE Class 9 Exam Overview: Syllabus, Sample Papers, and More
Class 9 under the Council for the Indian School Certificate Examinations (CISCE) is a crucial foundation year for students before they appear for the Class 10 ICSE Board Examination. While the final examinations are conducted internally by the individual affiliated schools and not by the CISCE board itself, the curriculum, exam pattern, and rigorous standards set by the board ensure students are well-prepared for the all-important Class 10 board exam.
1.0Key Features & Highlights of ICSE Class 9
Class 9 sets the tone for the Class 10 public examination, focusing on conceptual clarity and an extensive syllabus to develop strong analytical and practical skills.
2.0ICSE Class 9 Syllabus & Curriculum Structure
The ICSE curriculum in Class 9 is extensive and detailed. CISCE officially releases the syllabus and serves as the blueprint for the Class 10 curriculum. Students must download and follow the latest subject-wise syllabus from the official CISCE website.
The subject structure is divided into three groups:
3.0Core Syllabus Highlights (Examples)
- Mathematics: Includes topics like Pure Arithmetic, Commercial Mathematics, Algebra (Expansions, Factorisation, Logarithms), Geometry (Congruency, Pythagoras Theorem), Mensuration, Trigonometry, and Statistics.
- Science (Physics/Chemistry/Biology): Covers foundational concepts like Measurements, Motion, Laws of Motion, Light, Sound, Electricity (Physics); Language of Chemistry, Chemical Changes, Atomic Structure, Periodic Table (Chemistry); The Cell, Tissues, Plant Physiology, Human Anatomy (Biology).
- English Language: Focuses on Composition, Letter/Email/Notice writing, Unseen Passage, and Functional Grammar.
- English Literature: Involves the study of prescribed texts (plays, short stories, and poems) from a specific collection (e.g., The Merchant of Venice and Treasure Trove).
4.0Exam Pattern & Grading System
While the exact question paper format is set by the individual school for the final exam, the grading and assessment structure generally follows the ICSE pattern, which emphasises both theory and practical/internal assessment.
- Theory Paper: The structure often involves compulsory short-answer questions and a section requiring students to attempt a specific number of questions from a given choice, similar to the board exam pattern.
- Internal Assessment: This component is critical as it contributes 20% (for Group I & II) or 50% (for Group III) of the total marks and includes project work, assignments, practicals, and internal tests throughout the year.
5.0Study Materials: Sample Papers & Resources
Since Class 9 is a preparatory year, actively using relevant study materials is crucial for success.
- Textbooks: Strict adherence to the textbooks prescribed by CISCE/affiliated schools (e.g., Concise Selina for Science and Mathematics, Frank or Goyal for other subjects) is essential.
- Sample Papers and Question Banks: Solving ICSE Class 9 Sample Papers and Previous Years' Question Papers (PYQs) from the school or reputable publishers is the most effective preparation tool. This practice helps students:
- Familiarise themselves with the question paper pattern and the types of questions asked.
- Improve time management skills by simulating exam conditions.
- Identify their weak areas for focused revision.
- Selina Solutions/Reference Books: Using detailed solutions to textbook exercises helps in achieving conceptual clarity and mastering problem-solving techniques, especially in Mathematics and Science.
6.0Preparation Tips for Students
- Master the Syllabus: Since Class 9 forms the basis for Class 10, aim for deep conceptual clarity rather than just rote learning. Use the official syllabus as a checklist.
- Consistent Practice: Dedicate specific time for subjects like Mathematics and Science for problem-solving practice every day.
- Value Internal Marks: Take internal tests and project work seriously, as the marks obtained here will significantly contribute to your final Class 9 score.
- Systematic Revision: Due to the vastness of the syllabus, schedule weekly or monthly revision sessions to retain topics effectively.
- Develop Writing Speed: Practice writing comprehensive answers, especially for History/Civics and Geography, and work on the composition/letter/report writing sections in English.

NCERT Solutions for Class 7
As we begin preparing for the Class 7 exams, our primary focus often revolves around planning a study schedule and completing the course material. However, selecting the suitable study material is the most crucial aspect of effective exam preparation. Since CBSE and many state boards follow the NCERT curriculum, choosing NCERT Class 7 solutions is essential for thorough and effective preparation.
NCERT Solutions for Class 7 is a vital resource to help students excel in their studies. These solutions provide clear explanations and step-by-step answers to the questions in NCERT textbooks, ensuring that students fully grasp each concept. Covering subjects such as Mathematics, Science, Social Science, and English. Students can access 100% accurate, detailed solutions for every question in the most recent NCERT textbooks.
Here, we present expertly crafted NCERT Solutions for Class 7 for all subjects, along with tips on how to use them effectively for exam preparation.
1.0Subject-wise NCERT Solutions for Class 7
The NCERT Solutions for class 7 cover subjects such as Mathematics, Science, Social Science, and English. Keeping in mind the importance of every subject, we have come up with subject-wise NCERT Solutions for each subject of class 7, ensuring that these solutions are completely based on the latest syllabus and cover all the necessary concepts.
Find the list to download the subject-wise NCERT class 7 solution from the table given below:
2.0Detailed Breakdown of NCERT Solutions for Class 7
Let’s understand each subject of the NCERT Solutions for Class 7 in detail.
NCERT Solutions Class 7 Maths: These are crafted according to the CBSE curriculum for the academic session 2025-26.
Science NCERT Solutions for Class 7: The NCERT Solutions Class 7 Science are designed to simplify complex concepts and assist students in answering the questions and problems from their textbooks. These resources, available in book form, as online tools, or as study guides, aim to enhance students' understanding of the subject and help them master key concepts.
Social Science NCERT Solutions for Class 7: The NCERT Solutions for Class 9 SST provide students with a dependable platform to practice and review key topics, making them an excellent tool for exam preparation. These solutions guide students through each step to ensure a deep understanding of the content.
English NCERT Solutions for Class 7: In subjects like English, NCERT Solutions play a vital role in improving language proficiency by focusing on sentence construction, vocabulary, and grammar. These solutions are invaluable for students, teachers, and parents, providing a structured approach to mastering the subject.
3.0Importance of NCERT Class 7 Solutions
NCERT Class 7 Solutions play a crucial role in students' academic journeys, providing them with a solid foundation in various subjects. So here we have provided the reason to study with the NCERT Class 7 Solutions:
- NCERT Class 7 Solutions are created by experts who possess a deep understanding of the subjects. This means students get clear, straightforward answers to every question in their NCERT textbooks. Clear explanations of concepts help students understand complex concepts more easily, making learning both effective and enjoyable.
- These solutions are specifically designed to align with the CBSE curriculum precisely. This connection ensures that students are well-prepared for their exams, as most questions are based on the NCERT textbooks. By practising with these solutions, students can get used to the questions they might face, which helps increase their confidence and problem-solving abilities.
- Moreover, NCERT Solutions promote self-study and independent learning. Students can use these solutions whenever they have trouble, allowing them to learn at their own speed.
Overall, NCERT Class 7 Solutions are a valuable resource that helps students succeed academically and master essential subjects.
4.0How to Get Help from Class 7 NCERT Solutions in Exam Preparation?
- NCERT Class 7 solutions provide clear explanations and answers to all NCERT Textbooks questions, helping to better understand the Concepts.
- They are designed so that every detail, such as significant practice questions and exam format, will be included so that you will be prepared for the main exam.
- The solution is well-crafted with step-by-step instructions, so students can easily learn problem-solving techniques.
- You can use NCERT Class 7 Solutions to assess your understanding of the material. Try answering the questions on your own for a while, and then check your answers by looking at the solutions.
- It helps you learn from your mistakes because the solutions outline how to correct any you make.
- The Class 7 solutions cover all of the chapters in detail, ensuring that you are prepared for every subject before your tests.
- Using these solutions can increase your confidence in your ability to respond accurately and quickly to exam questions

CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers 2025-26
Class 7 is the foundational academic year in which students are introduced to complex concepts in all subjects, laying the groundwork for advanced learning. To make this transition smooth, CBSE Class 7 sample papers are a great tool for practice and revision. For achieving clearness in concepts or for exam preparation, these papers are an essential tool for all Class 7 students who desire to perform well. This is why we bring you CBSE Class 7 sample paper PDF links along with their solutions and pro tips to use them effectively.
1.0What Are CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers?
The CBSE Sample Papers for Class 7 are model question papers that mimic the actual examination scenario, which help students analyse their weak and strong areas and prepare effectively. In other words, the papers reflect real school examinations in terms of both structure and level. The papers in class VII contain a mix of different types of questions, like:
- Objective or MCQ-Based Question
- Short-answer Type Questions
- Long-answer Type Questions
- Diagram-based and
- Case-based questions
- Map-plotting (Only for Social Sciences)
2.0CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers All Subjects : Download PDF
In this section, students can download the 7th class CBSE sample paper PDF for All Subjects. These papers are curated according to the recent syllabus and guidelines of the Central Board of Secondary Education to provide complete and systematic practice:
CBSE Class 7 Maths Sample Papers
Topics are integers, fractions, algebra, basic equations, data handling, and geometry. These question papers aim to enhance speed, accuracy, and problem-solving effectiveness based on logical thinking.
CBSE Class 7 English Sample Papers
Sample papers include reading comprehension, grammar exercises, and writing exercises. As per prominent CBSE topics such as determiners, tenses, clauses, modals, and letter or story writing, they facilitate a command over language and expression.
CBSE Class 7 Science Sample Papers
Written from chapters like Nutrition, Heat, Acids and Bases, Physical and Chemical Changes, Reproduction, and Motion, these question papers facilitate scientific knowledge enhancement and practice of labelling diagrams.
CBSE Class 7 Social Science Sample Papers
Papers cover History (e.g., The Mughal Empire), Geography (e.g., Weather and Climate), and Civics (e.g., Equality in Democracy). They consist of map-based questions, analytical writing, and factual recall exercises.
Every topic-wise sample paper facilitates strategic preparation, enabling students to reinforce their knowledge and enhance performance.
3.0Importance of Practising Sample Papers
The following are some important points that articulate why CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers All Subjects are important for the students:
- Understand Exam Pattern: Sample papers mimic the trend, making students comfortable with question segmentation and mark distribution.
- Mitigate Exam Stress: Repeated practice makes them comfortable, lowering anxiety on exam day.
- Detecting Weak Areas: Post-test performance analysis identifies areas that require additional practice.
- Monitor Progress: Weekly solution of papers facilitates tracking improvement in speed, accuracy, and understanding.
- Enhance Writing Accuracy: Students improve their approach to presenting answers, framing responses, and using correct keywords.
4.0How Sample Papers Make Exam-Ready
CBSE class 7 sample paper PDFs are not only revision aids—they do work towards academic readiness. Here's why:
- Develop Logical Reasoning: Application-based and HOTS (Higher Order Thinking Skills) questions foster analytical abilities.
- Encourage Strategic Planning: Students are taught to distribute their time between sections and tackle higher-weightage questions first.
- Enhance Answer Framing: Repeated writing of answers hones sentence formation, particularly for language papers.
- Construct Consistency: Regular practice yields consistent improvement, leading to increased scores over time.
5.0The Right Time to Use These Exam Simulators
It is ideal to start practising once a student has covered 70% to 100% of the syllabus. For Term 1, practice typically begins in July or August, and for Term 2, it is ideal to start in December or January. Typically, practising with CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers is recommended 4–6 weeks before exams.
Start with chapter-wise sample questions once each topic is covered. As the exam nears, switch to full-length practice papers. These exams similarly aid in stamina building, minimising surprises, and enabling students to optimise their revision plan.
6.0How to Practice with CBSE Class 7 Sample Papers Effectively
Utilising CBSE sample paper class 7 PDFs without a plan underutilises them. Use this approach for maximum effectiveness:
- Develop a Weekly Practice Routine: Devote 2–3 sessions weekly to sample papers, tapering towards exams.
- Recreate Testing Conditions: Practice in a quiet area with a timer set, and attempt without pausing.
- Keep a Mistake Log: Log mistakes and misinterpretations to prevent repeating them.
- Revise Based on Performance: Spend extra time on topics where repeated errors are made for concentrated revision.
- Retake Tough Papers: Re-do earlier troublesome papers to assess improvement.
7.0Tips to Score High Using 7th Class CBSE Sample Paper
To derive maximum advantage from practising with CBSE Class 7 sample paper PDFs, adopt these tips:
- Do NCERT First: Practice with study resources, but always complete the NCERTs first for a core understanding of the chapters.
- Compare with Solutions: Compare your steps with solutions, if provided, for clarity and correctness.
- Practice Map and Diagram Skills: For Social Science and Science, add map-labelling and tidy diagrams.
- Answer to the Point: Do not give elaborate answers in short-type questions; be within the word limit.
- Improve Visual Presentation: Write clearly, use headings effectively, and create diagrams that are easy to follow to achieve high presentation marks.
- Discuss With Teachers: Report difficult questions or incorrect efforts to teachers to understand alternative strategies.
