1.0JEE Main 2026 Result Announcement Schedule
The JEE Main 2026 results for different sessions will be released separately by the National Testing Agency (NTA) and can be accessed at jeemain.nta.nic.in/ . The results for the January session will be released by February 16, 2026, and for the April session will be available by April 19, 2026(tentative).
Successful performance in the NTA JEE Main exam opens doors for candidates to secure admission into various prestigious institutions such as NITs, GFTIs, and other participating institutes.
To find out the specific result date and time for the IIT JEE Main 2026, please refer to the provided table:
In addition to the results, students will be able to review the JEE Main 2026 Answer Key before the announcement of results, enabling them to calculate their scores for the examination.
2.0Steps to Access and Download JEE Main 2026 Result
To access the JEE Main 2026 results, candidates are advised to follow the outlined steps:
- Navigate to the official website: jeemain.nta.nic.in.
- Locate and click on the link labeled "Result of JEE (Main)".
- Input your application number and date of birth, using the latter as the password.
- Click the "Submit" button to proceed.
- The JEE Main scorecard will be displayed on the screen for your review.
- Download the JEE Main 2026 result for future reference.
- Additionally, it is advisable to take a printout of the result for your records.
NTA ensures that a copy of the final scorecard will be sent to candidates via their registered email addresses. Be sure to check your email for this important document.
3.0Key Information Included in the JEE Main 2026 Scorecard
Please carefully review the provided information to ensure accuracy:
- Full Name of the Candidate
- Roll Number
- Date of Birth
- Parents' Names
- State Code of Eligibility
- Category and Nationality
- Individual NTA Scores for Each Subject
- Overall NTA Scores Attained in the Relevant Attempt
4.0JEE Main 2026 Result - How is Percentile Score Calculated?
The percentile score of a candidate is determined by calculating the percentage of candidates who scored equal to or below them in a given examination session. To compute this, one multiplies 100 by the ratio of the number of candidates with scores less than or equal to the candidate in question to the total number of candidates who participated in that session.
Understanding NTA JEE Main 2026 Percentile Score
The NTA JEE Main percentile indicates a candidate's relative performance compared to others, representing the percentage of candidates with lower scores.
Attaining the 100th percentile does not necessitate a perfect 100% score; it simply means having a higher score than all other candidates.
In this scenario, the candidate who secures the top position attains the 100th percentile since every other participant achieves a lower score than them.
5.0JEE Main Result - How Merit/Rank list is Prepared?
Ranks will be released once both the sessions of JEE Main are completed. For the candidates who sit for both sessions, the rank will be decided on the basis of the best of the scores obtained in the two sessions. The percentiles of both sessions will be calculated to the 7th decimal for all the students. It is done to avoid the tie of scores. For the candidates who sit for only one session, the rank will be decided based on their performances in that session.
6.0Tie-Breaking Procedures in JEE Main Result
- Initial tie-breaker compares scores in Mathematics. Higher score receives a superior rank.
- If Mathematics scores are identical, Physics scores are compared. A higher Physics score results in a better rank.
- In the case of matching Physics scores, Chemistry scores are evaluated. Higher Chemistry score leads to a higher rank.
- If Chemistry scores are also equal, the ratio of negative to positive responses is considered. A lower ratio merits a superior rank.
- As a final step, if all previous criteria fail, age becomes the deciding factor. The older candidate is awarded a better rank.
7.0Overview of the JEE Main Final Result
The NTA has introduced a two-session format for JEE Main, with result announcements after each session. The final result, combining scores from both sessions, will be declared after the conclusion of the second session. The comprehensive second session result is anticipated to include the following key information:
- Cumulative scores were obtained across both JEE Main sessions.
- The highest individual score was achieved among the two sessions.
- The result includes a qualifying cutoff, an admission cutoff, and a detailed rank list of candidates.
- Around 90th to 95th Percentile is ideal for getting admission to IITs and NITs.
- JEE Main exam conducted in 13 languages, viz. Gujarati, Hindi, Assamese, Bengali, English, Kannada, Odia, Punjabi, Malayalam, Marathi, Tamil, Telugu, and Urdu.
8.0JEE Main Participating Institutes
Here is the list of all the participating institutes in JEE Main 2025 with the number of seats available.
9.0Facility for Re-Evaluation of JEE Mains Results 2026
As per the guidelines provided by the National Testing Agency (NTA) for JEE Main, there will be no opportunity for result re-evaluation or re-checking. The scores disclosed in the results will be considered conclusive, and no provisions for review or modification will be entertained.

Mathematics
Mathematics is the study of patterns, structure, and relationships, rooted in fundamental practices like counting, measuring, and analyzing shapes. It combines logical reasoning with quantitative calculations, evolving from practical applications to highly abstract concepts. Over centuries, Mathematics has become essential in various fields, from physical sciences and technology to life sciences, playing a crucial role in understanding and solving complex problems.
Mathematics is essential for both academic success and everyday life. It fosters mental discipline and encourages logical reasoning. While some students may find math challenging, ALLEN offers an engaging approach that helps all grade levels learn effectively.
Students can also check for revision notes and solved papers to support further practice, helping them excel in their math skills. Ultimately, Math promotes critical thinking and contributes to healthy brain development.
1.0List of All Maths Articles
This list of Maths articles covers a wide range of topics from basic to advanced levels, helping students consolidate important concepts in one place. You can access detailed explanations and solved examples by clicking on each article.
2.0History of Mathematics
History shows that the central ideas of Mathematics have been developed over thousands of years, starting with ancient civilizations, such as the Babylonians and Egyptians, who used elementary arithmetic and geometry for such practical purposes as commerce and building. The Greeks, Euclid and Archimedes, furnished the underpinning to formal mathematical argument by introducing the use of proof, and developing both geometry and number theory.
In the Middle Ages, Islamic scholars not only preserved Greek mathematics but also extended it significantly in two very critical topics for students: algebra and trigonometry.
The 19th and 20th centuries saw the development of abstract fields of Set theory, Abstract Algebra, and Topology, with the formalization of Mathematical Logic. These fields continue to turn the wheels in Mathematics at the higher level. Today, Mathematics is the vehicle driving advances in Technology, Physics, and many other subjects—hence a critical tool for success in any exam, and in understanding the world.
3.0Fundamental Aspects of Mathematics
- Numbers and Arithmetic: Mathematics starts with basic concepts like numbers (natural numbers, integers, rational numbers, real numbers, and complex numbers) and arithmetic operations (addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division).
- Algebra: Algebra involves manipulating symbols and expressions to solve equations and understand relationships between variables. It includes topics like polynomials, linear equations, and quadratic equations.
- Geometry: Geometry studies shapes, sizes, and the properties of space. It ranges from basic concepts of points, lines, and angles to more complex topics like the properties of polygons, circles, and three-dimensional figures.
- Calculus: Calculus focuses on change and motion. It includes differential calculus (studying rates of change and slopes of curves) and integral calculus (examining accumulation of quantities and areas under curves).
- Statistics and Probability: Statistics involves collecting, analyzing, and interpreting data, while probability theory studies the likelihood of events occurring. These fields are essential for making informed decisions based on data.
- Number Theory: Number theory explores the properties and relationships of numbers, particularly integers. It includes the study of prime numbers, divisibility rules, and modular arithmetic.
- Discrete Mathematics: Discrete mathematics deals with distinct and separate values, often involving topics like graph theory, combinatorics, and logic. It's crucial for computer science and algorithm design.
- Mathematical Reasoning and Proof: Mathematics relies heavily on logical reasoning and proof to establish the truth of statements. Various proof techniques, such as direct proof, induction, and contradiction, are used to validate mathematical propositions.

JEE Main 2026 Papers Analysis: January Session 1 (Shift 1 and Shift 2)
Get ahead with our thorough analysis of the JEE Main Papers, based on detailed student feedback from the January and April sessions. This analysis clearly summarises class-wise questions and difficulty levels, providing essential insights for future exams. By understanding these elements, you can refine your study approach, focus on key areas, and significantly improve your performance. 1.0JEE Main 2026 Paper Analysis - Session 1 Detailed Analysis and Video
Download Here: JEE Main 2026 Session 1 Solved Shiftwise Papers
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 28 Shift Wise Question Paper Analysis - Subject Wise
The overall paper was moderate in the morning session
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 28 Shift 1 (Morning Shift)
Physics
The Physics paper was easy, with most questions based on formulae. 40% of the questions were from the 11th-grade syllabus, and 60% from the 12th-grade syllabus. Questions primarily asked from the topics Mechanics, Electrodynamics, and Heat and Thermodynamics sections.
Chemistry
The Chemistry paper was moderate but lengthy. Most of the questions were statement-type. Physical Chemistry included two questions each from Atomic Structure and Ionic Equilibrium, Aromatic Compounds and Hydrocarbons in Organic Chemistry, and Coordination Compounds in Inorganic Chemistry.
Maths
The Math paper was moderate and lengthy. Except for a few difficult questions, students solved most of the questions easily. Algebra comprised 36 % of the paper, followed by Calculus 32 %, Coordinate Geometry 16 %, Vector 3D 12 %, and Trigonometry 4 %.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 24 Shift Wise Question Paper Analysis - Subject Wise
The paper in the morning shift was overall moderate and lengthy.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 24 Shift 1 (Morning Shift)
Physics
Physics paper was moderate and calculation-based. Mostly questions were asked from topics- Electrodynamics and Mechanics, Geometry, Optics, Heat, and Thermodynamics.
Chemistry
Chemistry paper was moderate. Physical and Organic Chemistry questions carried a higher weightage. 34% of the questions were from the 11th-grade syllabus, and 56% from the 12th-grade syllabus.
Maths
Math paper was moderate and lengthy. Nine questions were asked from algebra, eight from calculus, four from coordinate geometry, and two each from vector 3D and trigonometry.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 24 Shift 2 (Evening Shift)
Physics
Physics paper was moderate.
Chemistry
Chemistry paper was very calculation-heavy and had tricky questions.
Maths
Maths paper was easy to moderate but very lengthy.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 23 Shift Wise Question Paper Analysis - Subject Wise
The paper in the morning shift was overall difficult and lengthy.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 23 Shift 1 (Morning Shift)
Physics
The Physics paper in the morning shift was of moderate difficulty. The number of questions asked from Mechanics, Magnetism, and Electrodynamics topics was higher. 40% of the questions in the paper were from the 11th-grade syllabus and 60% from the 12th-grade syllabus.
Chemistry
The Chemistry paper in the morning shift was difficult. In Physical Chemistry, students took more time because the answer could only be obtained after calculating all the options in a single question. Two questions each were asked from Coordination Chemistry, Aromatic Compounds, and Chemical Equilibrium. 40% of the questions in the paper were from the 11th-grade syllabus and 60% from the 12th-grade syllabus.
Maths
The Maths paper in the morning shift was of moderate difficulty. The number of questions from Algebra and Calculus was higher. The paper included 44% of the questions from the 11th-grade syllabus and 56% from the 12th-grade syllabus.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 23 Shift 2 (Evening Shift)
The overall paper was tougher compared to all previous shifts.
Physics
Physics was the tough and lengthy.
Chemistry
Chemistry was moderate and included several statement-based questions.
Maths
Maths was easy and lengthy.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 22 Shift Wise Question Paper Analysis - Subject Wise
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 22 Shift 1 (Morning Shift)
As per students reaction, overall paper in the January 22 shift was easier than January 21 exam. It was moderate to difficult level.
Physics
Physics was Moderate to Tough. Most of the questions were asked from Electrodynamics, Mechanics, Modern Wave, Magnetism, Geometrical Optics, and Heat and Thermodynamics.
Chemistry
Chemistry was Difficult and Lengthy. Questions from Inorganic and Organic Chemistry kept the students engaged. The calculations in the Physical Chemistry paper were also difficult. 28% of the questions were covered from Inorganic Chemistry and 36-36% from Organic and Physical Chemistry.
Maths
Maths paper was Moderate level and Lengthy. 32% of the questions were from Algebra and Calculus each, and the remaining questions were from Vector 3D, Trigonometry, and Coordinate Geometry.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 22 Shift 2 (Evening Shift)
As per the JEE Main 2026 January 22 Shift 2 question paper analysis, the Mathematics section was difficult, Chemistry was of moderate difficulty, and Physics ranged from easy to moderate. Overall, the Shift 2 paper was moderate to tough.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 21 Shift Wise Question Paper Analysis - Subject Wise
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 21 Shift 1
Overall, the JEE Main 2026 January 21 Session 1 paper was rated as moderate in difficulty. Chemistry and Physics were moderate, and Math was lengthy.
Physics
The Physics paper was moderate. Mostly questions were from electrodynamics, mechanics, heat and thermodynamics, waves and wave optics, magnetism, gravitation, modern physics, geometry, optics, fluids, errors, and semiconductors.
Chemistry
The Chemistry paper was moderate. The calculations in Section B were difficult. The questions covered almost the entire syllabus, with two questions each from chemical bonding, salt analysis, aromatic compounds, and mole concepts. One question each appeared from all other topics.
Maths
The Maths paper was difficult and lengthy. Algebra comprised 36 %, Calculus 28%, Vector 3D 12%, Trigonometry 8%, and Coordinates 16%.
JEE Mains 2026 Jan 21 Shift 2
The shift 2 of the JEE Main 21 2026 exam is now over. The overall exam was more difficult than the morning shift.
Physics
Physics was Moderate. The Physics paper in the evening shift was moderate, with questions from mechanics, wave optics, electrodynamics, modern physics, magnetism, and fluids.
Chemistry
In the evening shift, 24% of the questions were from inorganic chemistry, 36% from organic chemistry, and 40% from physical chemistry. Due to the calculations involved, the evening shift paper was more difficult than the morning paper.
Maths
The paper in the evening shift was tough in which 32% questions were asked from Algebra, 32% from Calculus, 12% from Vector 3D, 8% from Trigonometry, 16% from Coordinate Geometry.

Maths: Paper was more difficult than morning shift. Here, 32% questions were asked from Calculus, 32% from Algebra, 16% from Coordinates, 12% from 3D Vectors and 4% each from Trigonometry and Statistics and Relation.

CBSE Class 10 Sample Papers with Solutions
In preparation for any exam, practising sample papers is crucial. The more you practice, the better you understand the subject and its concepts, leading to higher scores. Therefore, as you prepare for the Class 10 CBSE board exam, you must enhance your grasp of the subjects by practising with sample papers from the latest and previous years. These sample papers are meticulously crafted to reflect the latest CBSE class 10 exam pattern, providing a realistic preview of the exam. The CBSE Board has uploaded the sample papers for the 2024-25 session on the official website.
This article compiles Class 10 CBSE sample papers for class 10 for all subjects, crafted by ALLEN subject matter experts, and also by the officials to help you excel and achieve high scores.

Additional Study Material:
Most questions in past Class 10 CBSE exams have been based on NCERT content. It is important to stay informed about the latest changes in NCERT. However, a common question is where to download these papers. What is the best platform to access the CBSE Sample Papers for Class 10? Access a comprehensive set of additional subject-wise sample papers for Class 10 CBSE exams prepared by ALLEN’s expert faculty. These papers are meticulously designed to match the board exam pattern, providing students with targeted practice and a clear understanding of the exam questions they may encounter so that they can boost their confidence on exam day.
7.0How to Download Official CBSE Class 10 Sample Papers?
You can easily download the CBSE Class 10 sample papers in PDF format from the official website by following these steps:
- Go to official website: cbseacademic.nic.in.
- Look for and click on the ‘Sample Question Paper’ option on the homepage.
- Choose the ‘SQP year’ from the options provided.
- Then, select ‘Class 10’ from the drop-down menu.
- The sample question paper PDF and the marking scheme are on your screen.
- Click on the ‘Download’ button to save the CBSE sample paper for Class 10 as a PDF file on your device.
8.0Benefits of Solving CBSE Class 10 Sample Papers
Practising sample question papers before exams can significantly increase your chances of achieving favourable outcomes. Here are the benefits of solving Class 10 Sample Question Papers:
- Sample papers provide insights into the upcoming Board Exams, including the format, question types, and marking scheme.
- Reviewing sample question papers can help you gain insights into commonly asked questions, chapters with high frequency, and question types.
- Regularly practising sample question papers helps you gauge your speed in answering questions, leading to improved time management during exams.
- Sample papers help identify weak areas, allowing you to focus on them and improve before exams, ultimately leading to higher scores.
- Your self-confidence will increase as you become more familiar with the content in sample papers and answer questions without fear.
- Practising sample papers can help reduce anxiety and exam stress as you gain insights into the exam structure and become more prepared.

JEE Advanced Rank Predictor 2025
The JEE (Advanced) Rank Predictor helps candidates anticipate their rank based on their exam scores. To appear on the JEE Advanced 2025 rank list, candidates must achieve a minimum aggregate percentage set by the exam authorities. Candidates can use the JEE Advanced 2025 answer key, available at ALLEN, to calculate their scores. By analyzing their results and ranks using the answer key, candidates can understand their performance and prepare for their next plans.
1.0JEE Advanced 2025 Rank Predictor - How to Calculate the Score?
Candidates need the JEE Advanced answer key and marking scheme to calculate the exact scores. Here's are the steps on how to calculate scores:
1. Download the answer key from the JEE Advanced official website.
2. Compare responses with the answer key and refer to the marking scheme.
3. Calculate the score and note down the marks obtained.
4. The result of this calculation is your probable JEE Advanced score.
After your score calculation you can predict your rank using JEE Advanced rank predictor tool and prepare yourself for next step.
2.0Key Features of JEE Advanced 2025 Rank Predictor
Below are the following key features of the predictor tool.
- Early Insights: Candidates will be able to know their predicted rank even before the official results come out.
- Strategic Planning: Gain valuable insights into your potential rank early in the admission process, allowing you to strategize effectively for counselling and seat allocation.
- User-friendly: Access the rank predictor effortlessly using basic details.
- Exam Analysis: The JEE Advanced predictor provides exam analysis to candidates.

CBSE Class 10 Date Sheet [OUT]
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) will conduct the Class 10 exams for the academic year 2025–26 from 17th February 2026 to 11th March 2026 in a pen-and-paper format. The Final date sheet has been released on the official cbse.gov.in on 30th October 2025, which includes exam dates, timings, and important instructions for all subjects. In this article, you can find the detailed CBSE Class 10 Exam Date Sheet 2025–26.
Exam Dates: 17th February 2026 to 11th March 2026
Download New Guide Lines for Class 10 Practical Exams
1.0CBSE Class 10 Date Sheet 2025-26: Know about the date and timing
2.0CBSE Class 10 Exam Date 2025 - Highlights
3.0Beyond the Date Sheet: Preparing for Success
Although the class 10 board CBSE date sheet is your guide to study, routine work and dedicated study will decide your success. Following are some important points for proper preparation:
- Understand the syllabus: Read every subject's CBSE Class 10 syllabus thoroughly. Identify the major topics and concepts which are crucial for the exam.
- Read study guides and textbooks: Prepare for the examinations using the prescribed study guide and textbooks. Other documents, such as previous year's question papers and sample papers, can also be used.
- Practice regularly: Practice is the key to learning any subject. Practice question papers and previous year's question papers to familiarize yourself with the exam pattern and identify your weaknesses and strengths.
- Conceptual knowledge: Don't memorize the numbers and facts. Understanding concepts will enable you to answer questions more effectively.
- Seek clarification: Whenever you are confused or uncertain, don't fear to ask teachers or classmates for clarification. Clarifying doubts is important to building a strong foundation.
- Remain motivated: Remain motivated during the preparation process. Set achievable goals and treat yourself to a reward upon accomplishing them.
- Manage stress: Exam time can be stressful. Try relaxation techniques such as meditation and deep breathing to help manage stress and anxiety.

CBSE Class 8 Sample Papers
CBSE Class 8 sample papers have one simple goal - turning guesswork into confidence. In class 8, there are more layers in the subject, and in order to excel in the exam, you need to do more than just read your textbooks. Whether you are trying to understand colonial history in Social Science, solving algebra in Math, or writing crisp essays in English, there is a lot to cover.
Solving CBSE sample paper for Class 8 PDFs for every subject will prepare you for the exam. They are like a preview of what to come, giving you the skills, tools, and confidence to handle your exam like a pro.
1.0CBSE Class 8 Sample Papers – Introduction
You spend hours going through the chapters, learning every important fact and definition, and getting an in-depth understanding of the concepts. However, the real challenge is whether you can write what you know clearly and correctly during the exam.
Thai is where CBSE class 8 sample papers come in. They are a must-have in your prep toolkit as they mirror the actual CBSE exams. This way, you can:
- Get used to the question types
- Practice writing under time pressure, and
- Learn how to frame high-scoring answers.
Through these sample papers, you will be able to cover all the major subjects, including Maths, Science, Social Science, English and Hindi. With these CBSE Class 8 Sample Papers All Subjects, you are not just memorising, you are mastering.
2.0Download Subject-wise Sample papers for CBSE Class 8: Free PDF
3.0Download Class 8 Sample Papers with Solutions – Free PDF
Studying smarter is better than studying harder. To score high in your exam, you need to make use of every single weapon in your arsenal.
With just a click, you can download 8th Class CBSE Sample Paper PDF files, completely free. These are created by top subject experts, updated regularly, and aligned with the latest CBSE curriculum.
Here’s what you get:
- Full-length, exam-style papers
- A perfect mix of objective and subjective questions
- Detailed solutions that explain the how and why behind each answer
Whether you prefer practising on paper or solving digitally, these CBSE sample paper class 8 PDF files are flexible and easy to use.
4.0Previous Year Class 8 Sample Papers with Solutions PDF
Want to know what kinds of questions really show up in exams? Look no further than previous year papers.
Our CBSE class 8 sample paper PDF collection includes actual sample questions from past sessions. These give you:
- A sneak peek into the real exam format
- Insights into frequently asked topics
- A head start on the most important chapters
And yes, you’ll get complete solutions too. So you’re never left wondering where you went wrong or how to make your answers better. Think of it like a friendly mentor guiding you after every test run.
5.0How Sample Papers Are Different from Other Study Resources
You already have your textbooks, chapter summaries, and maybe a few online tutorials. So, why add sample papers?
Because they give you something none of the others do: a real exam experience.
Solving a CBSE class 8 sample paper is like stepping into the exam hall before the actual day. You get to:
- Practice managing your time,
- Answer full-length question sets, and
- Feel the pressure and learn to beat it.
Textbooks tell you what to learn. Sample papers show you how to perform. That’s the key difference. And because our CBSE Class 8 Sample Papers All Subjects are aligned with current CBSE guidelines and marking schemes, they help you train exactly the way CBSE expects.
6.0Other Essential Study Materials for CBSE Class 8 Exam 2025–26
Want to take your preparation from good to great? Mix in these essential study tools with your sample papers:
When used alongside CBSE Class 8 sample paper PDFs, these materials create a complete, bulletproof prep routine.
7.0Why You Should Practice CBSE Class 8 Sample Papers PDF with Solutions
Practising with CBSE Class 8 sample papers is like giving your brain a workout before the big exam. It sharpens your thinking, improves your writing, and helps you stay calm under pressure. Here’s a closer look at why these sample papers are your best friend during exam prep:
1. Boosts Your Exam Confidence: The fear of exams often comes from uncertainty. What kind of questions will appear? Will I be able to finish on time? Solving sample papers helps remove that fear. The more papers you attempt, the more familiar the pattern becomes, making the final exam feel like just another practice round.
2. Teaches Time Management: Many students know the answers but struggle to finish their papers on time. Practising sample papers under a timer helps you figure out how long to spend on each section. You’ll develop a rhythm, so you don’t leave any questions incomplete.
3. Sharpens Your Concepts: It’s one thing to read a chapter, and another to explain it in your own words. Sample papers test your understanding by asking application-based questions. This forces you to think deeper and builds long-term memory instead of just surface-level learning.
4. Perfects Your Answer Format: CBSE gives marks not only for correct content but also for presentation. Solutions in sample papers teach you how to structure your answers, where to use bullet points, headings, introductions, and how to conclude properly. This polish makes a huge difference, especially in long-answer questions.
5. Spot Your Weaknesses: Your weak areas don’t always show up in casual study sessions. But when you solve full-length papers, you’ll start seeing patterns, topics where you make the most mistakes or take too long. Once you identify those, you can revisit those chapters with targeted revision.
6. Improves Critical Thinking: Subjects like Science and Social Science require more than rote memorisation. You need to analyse cause and effect, compare data, and explain logic clearly. Regular practice helps build these thinking skills, making your answers deeper and more thoughtful.
7. Provides Instant Feedback: Most students wait for a teacher to check their answers, but with the 8th Class CBSE sample paper that comes with step-by-step solutions, you get immediate feedback. This allows you to self-correct, understand your mistakes, and improve right away, without having to wait for a classroom evaluation.

CBSE Question Papers

These papers allow for effective self-assessment, helping you gauge how many questions may come from each chapter. To score high, consistent practice and thorough revision are crucial, both of which can be achieved through CBSE Notes and Previous Year Question Papers. CBSE Notes provide a quick way to revise concepts, while solving past question papers helps you familiarize yourself with important topics, marking schemes, and sometimes repeated questions in the exam. This comprehensive approach ensures better preparation and boosts your chances of excelling in the exams.
You can find CBSE Question Papers for Class 10 and Class 12 in this article, so that you understand the pattern clearly and the types of questions which are likely to appear in the 2025-26 exams. As for subjects like Mathematics, English, and Science, consistent practice will be relevant for the theory as well as for the problem-solving techniques.

JEE Main Eligibility Criteria
JEE Main Eligibility Criteria 2026 - Overview
The National Testing Agency (NTA) has released the JEE Main 2026 examination dates and eligibility criteria on its website, jeemain.nta.nic.in. The exam will occur over two sessions: Session 1 will take place from January 21 to January 29, 2026, and Session 2 will take place from April 2 to April 09, 2026. The key aspects of JEE Main 2026 eligibility criteria will include educational qualifications, age limit, nationality, marks obtained in Class 12, compulsory subjects, and so on. This article details all important points relating to age criteria, number of attempts, minimum eligibility marks and percentage pass mark. Candidates are advised to check and understand the updated eligibility requirements of JEE Main 2026 to stay up to date on the notifications.
1.0JEE Main Examination 2026 Eligibility Criteria - Highlights
Examination Name | Joint Entrance Examination Main (JEE Main) |
Conducting Body | National Testing Agency (NTA) |
Purpose | Gateway to NITs, IIITs, and CFTIs; Qualifying test for JEE Advanced |
Category | National Level Undergraduate (UG) Exam |
Level of Exam | Rigorous National Examination |
Number of Sessions | Two |
Mode of Exam | Paper 1 (B.E/B.Tech) - Computer-Based Test (CBT) Paper 2A (B.Arch.) - CBT (except Drawing Test) Paper 2B (B.Plan.) - Computer-Based Test (CBT)+A26:A27 |
Mode of Application | Online |
Official Website | jeemain.nta.nic.in |
2.0Qualifying Examination Criteria for JEE Main 2026
To be eligible for JEE Main 2026, candidates must have successfully completed one of the following examinations:
- The final examination of the 10+2 system is conducted by recognized Central/ State Boards like CBSE, CISCE, etc.
- Intermediate or two-year Pre-University examination conducted by a recognized Board/ University.
- Final examination of the two-year course of the Joint Services Wing of the National Defense Academy.
- Senior Secondary School Examination conducted by the National Institute of Open Schooling with a minimum of five subjects.
- Any Public School/ Board/ University examination in India or abroad recognized as equivalent to the 10+2 system by the Association of Indian Universities (AIU).
- Higher Secondary Certificate Vocational Examination.
- A Diploma recognized by AICTE or a State board of technical education with a duration of at least three years.
- General Certificate Education (GCE) examination (London/Cambridge/Sri Lanka) at the Advanced (A) level.
- High School Certificate Examination of the Cambridge University or International Baccalaureate Diploma of the International Baccalaureate Office, Geneva.
3.0Nationality Requirement for JEE Main 2026
Candidates applying for JEE Main 2026 must hold Indian citizenship. Additionally, foreign nationals, as well as Non-Resident Indians (NRIs), Persons of Indian Origin (PIOs), and Overseas Citizens of India (OCIs), are also eligible to apply.
4.0Age Limitation in JEE Main 2026 Eligibility Criteria
There is no specific age limit for candidates appearing in JEE Main 2026. Individuals who have successfully completed their class 12 or equivalent examination in 2024, 2025, or are currently enrolled and set to complete it in 2026, irrespective of their age, are eligible to participate in the JEE Main 2026 examination.
5.0Number of Attempts Allowed JEE Main 2026
Aspiring candidates for JEE Main 2026 must meet specific eligibility criteria regarding the number of attempts. Those who have completed their class 12 in 2024 or 2025 or are currently in the process of appearing for the class 12 exams in 2026, are eligible to participate in JEE Main 2026. However, candidates who completed their class 12 before 2023 or are scheduled to appear in class 12 after 2026 are not eligible for the exam. It's worth noting that JEE Main 2026 will be conducted twice in January and April.
6.0Marks Eligibility Criteria for JEE Main 2026
In terms of marks eligibility, candidates falling under the general category must secure a minimum of 75% in their Class 12 board exams or rank among the top 20 percentile in their qualifying examination to meet the minimum marks to qualify JEE Mains 2026. This criterion is essential for admission to IITs, NITs, IIITs, and other CFTIs.
For candidates belonging to reserved categories, a minimum of 65% marks is required to gain admission to various undergraduate courses offered at IITs, NITs, IIITs, and other CFTIs. This ensures a fair and inclusive selection process based on academic performance.
7.0Subject Requirements in Class 12 for JEE Main Eligibility
The JEE Main 2026 is designed for three distinct courses - B.Tech, B.Arch, and B.Plan - each with specific subject requirements.
- For JEE Main 2026 Paper 1 (B.Tech.):
Candidates must have Physics and Mathematics as mandatory subjects, along with one additional subject from Chemistry, Biology, or a Technical Vocational Subject.
- For JEE Main 2026 Paper 2A (B.Arch.):
Successful completion of Class 12 or an equivalent examination with Physics, Chemistry, and Mathematics is a prerequisite.
- For JEE Main 2026 Paper 2B (B.Plan.):
Candidates are required to have Mathematics as a subject in their Class 12 or equivalent examination.
8.0State Code of Eligibility Criteria
The state code of eligibility in JEE Main 2026 pertains to the state from which a candidate has successfully completed their Class 12 (or equivalent) qualifying examination, enabling them to participate in the exam. It's important to note that the state code of eligibility is not contingent upon the candidate's native place or current residence.
To illustrate, if a candidate completes their Class 12 exam in New Delhi but resides in Noida, Uttar Pradesh, their state code of eligibility will be Delhi, not Uttar Pradesh.
Similarly, for candidates who pass the Class 12 qualifying exam in one state but pursue improvement from another, the state code of eligibility is determined by the state where the candidate initially passed their Class 12 exam, not the state of improvement.
For candidates who have passed or appeared for the Class 12 exam through the National Institute of Open Schooling (NIOS), the state of eligibility should be selected based on the location of the study center.
Indian nationals who have completed their Class 12 exam in Nepal/Bhutan will have their state code of eligibility determined by the permanent address mentioned in their passport.
For Overseas Citizens of India (OCI) and Persons of Indian Origin (PIO) who have completed their Class 12 exam in India, the state code of eligibility aligns with that of Indian nationals. However, OCI/PIO candidates completing the Class 12 exam from an institution abroad are eligible for Other State quota seats or All India quota seats (excluding Home State quota seats) in all NITs, IIEST, and other Centrally Funded Technical Institutions (CFTIs).

9.0Year of Appearance Eligibility in JEE Main 2026
As per the eligibility criteria for JEE Main 2026, candidates must have successfully completed their Class 12 or an equivalent examination in the years 2024, 2025, or be currently enrolled and appearing in the 2026 examination. It's important to note that individuals who completed their Class 12 or equivalent examination in 2023 or earlier, as well as those set to appear in 2027 or beyond, are not eligible for JEE Main 2026.
10.0Eligibility for JEE Main 2026 for Droppers
Age Limit | No specific age limit for repeaters. |
Educational Qualification | Passed the class 12 exam in 2024, 2025, or 2026. |
Attempt Limit | Maximum of three consecutive attempts allowed for JEE Main. |
Minimum Qualification Marks | Candidates must secure the minimum required qualification marks for JEE Mains. |
Subject Requirement | Should have studied at least five subjects in the Class 12 or qualifying exam. Mandatory subjects include Physics, Chemistry, and Mathematics. |
11.0Eligibility Criteria for Appearing in JEE Advanced through JEE Main
JEE Main Participation:
Candidates must have participated in JEE Main and attained the minimum cutoff marks set by NTA.
Top 2,50,000 Rank:
As per the Common Rank List, candidates need to secure a position in the top 2,50,000 ranks for eligibility in JEE Advanced Exam 2026.
Attempt Limit:
Candidates are permitted to appear in JEE Advanced twice, consecutively in the two years following the year of passing the class 12 or equivalent examination.

JEE Advanced 2026 Exam Date: Check Complete Official Schedule
Students all over India who want to get into IITs are looking forward to the JEE Advanced 2026 exam dates. Conducted by one of the IITs on a rotational basis, this prestigious engineering entrance exam will take place on Sunday, May 17, 2026. It is now official that IIT Roorkee will be the organizing institute of the JEE Advanced 2026 exam. The exam will be held in two sessions: Paper 1 is scheduled from 9:00 AM to 12:00 PM IST, and Paper 2 will take place from 2:30 PM to 5:30 PM IST.
Aspiring JEE candidates can stay informed about the latest news and updates for JEE Advanced 2026 right here. Keeping track of these updates will enable candidates to plan their preparation effectively and avoid any last-minute stress. In this article, we provide the latest updates on the JEE Advanced 2026 exam schedule, including expected dates for registration, admit card release, exam day, and result declaration.
1.0JEE Advanced 2026: Important Dates
The detailed examination schedule for the JEE Advanced Exam in 2026 will be available at jeeadv.ac.in. The important dates listed below help candidates stay prepared.
2.0JEE Advanced 2026 Exam Dates
The Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Roorkee announced that the JEE Advanced 2026 exam will be held on 17th May 2026 (Sunday). ALLEN will provide the updated dates on this page and will update more details regarding the application form as soon as it is released on the official website - jeeadv.ac.in/.
Both Paper 1 and Paper 2 of the JEE Advanced Exams are scheduled on the same day. Paper 1 will be from 9:00 a.m. to 12:00 p.m., and Paper 2 will be conducted from 2:30 p.m. to 5:30 p.m.
3.0JEE Advanced 2026 Application Form Date
Candidates must thoroughly review the JEE Advanced eligibility criteria before filling out the form. Any inaccuracies in the application form or failure to meet the JEE Advanced criteria may result in the rejection of their application.
4.0JEE Advanced 2026 Application Correction Date
As per the latest update, there is no provision for application form correction. Candidates should stay updated with the latest official notifications and guidelines for any changes or clarifications related to the application process.
5.0JEE Advanced Admit Card Date
Organising body will release the JEE Advanced Admit Card on the official JEE Advanced website. After the admit card is released, candidates can download it by entering their JEE Advanced 2026 registration number, date of birth, and mobile number. The admit card will contain important details such as the candidate's name, roll number, photograph, signature, examination centre, and exam day instructions.
6.0JEE Advanced Answer Key Date
The exam conducting IIT will display the provisional answer keys and JEE Advanced 2026 answer key after the examination. These answer keys will be accessible through the official JEE Advanced website, allowing candidates to view them. Candidates can use the provisional answer keys to estimate their expected scores. In case of any discrepancies, candidates can raise challenges by paying a fee, and the final answer key will be released after resolving all such challenges along with the results.
7.0Result Date of JEE Advanced 2026
After the results are released, candidates can access them by logging in to the JEE Advanced website. The results will include ranks in the Common Rank List (CRL) and All India Rank (AIR) according to categories. Candidates who successfully log in can also download their results for future reference, as no physical copy of the marks card will be sent to them by post.
That’s all for the important dates related to the JEE Advanced exam 2026.
JEE Advanced 2026 candidates must be willing to prepare and take the exam with all they have. Moreover, this calls for proper guidance, preparation, and hard work. Every year lakhs of candidates attempt the JEE (Mains) and JEE (Advanced), but only those who have studied hard are able to crack the exam in one go.

CBSE Class 10 Exam
CBSE Class 10 Exam is a milestone in a student's academic journey. It is a turning point that lays the foundation for future success. As the CBSE board exams serve as a benchmark for progress, it is essential for students to have access to comprehensive and top-notch learning resources.
1.0CBSE Class 10 Exams 2025-26 Question Papers with Solutions PDF: Video Solutions
The Central Board of Secondary Education's (CBSE) Class 10th board exams will be conducted from February 17th to March 11 2026. We at ALLEN have provided a direct link to download the question papers in PDF format for free.
Class 10 Maths Question Paper 2026 with Solutions (Basic and Advanced)
Download CBSE Class 10 Board Exams 2026 Solved Question Papers PDF
Class 10 Previous year Question Papers 2024-25 - Download PDF
Students will greatly benefit from a systematic and organised approach to optimising their studies in class 10. Our blog guides and engages Class 10 CBSE students, providing valuable insights, tips, and strategies to make the most of their learning experience.
At ALLEN, we recognize the importance of excelling in this pivotal stage of education. That's why we offer class 10 study material packages available at ALLEN estore, designed explicitly for CBSE Class 10 subjects, including Math, Science, English, Hindi, and Social Science.
2.0Overview of CBSE Class 10 Exam
Class 10 exams are crucial in a student's academic journey, marking a significant transition point. These exams, especially the CBSE board exams, are not just assessments but are essential in setting the groundwork for future academic and career prospects. The results from these exams play a pivotal role in determining the educational streams and opportunities available to students as they progress to higher secondary education.
These exams test a student's grasp of fundamental concepts, critical thinking, and problem-solving abilities. ALLEN offers a comprehensive selection of free online learning resources for CBSE Class 10 subjects, including Math, Science, English, Hindi, and Social Science, to support students in this vital stage. These resources include CBSE Solutions, past question papers, detailed revision notes, syllabus outlines, and sample papers, all crafted by qualified educators.
Highlights of CBSE Class 10 2025-26 Exam
Discover key Class 10 Board Exam highlights to help you organise your study schedule and understand essential exam terms.
3.0CBSE Class 10 Exam Dates 2025-26
The Tentative CBSE Class 10 Exam dates for the Academic year 2025-26 are released on the CBSE Official website on September 24, 2025: Phase 1 - Main Exam from February 17 to March 9, 2026, and Phase 2 - (Optional) - Improvement Exam from May 15 to June 1, 2026. and the first phase is mandatory for all students, while the second phase is optional for those looking to improve their scores.
The official date sheet includes both regular and private student exams. Students can download the detailed format for future reference. Here is a brief table of the latest exam dates for CBSE Class 10 Exam Dates 2025-26:
Details in the CBSE Class 10 Date Sheet
The CBSE Class 10 exam date sheet provides students with essential information. It is a comprehensive guide for students to prepare and plan their study schedules.
- Names of the Subjects: List which subjects will be tested for clarity.
- Subject Codes: Unique codes for each subject to ensure accurate identification.
- Important Examination Dates: Key dates for each subject's 2025 CBSE Class 10th Board exam examination.
- Other Exam Schedule Details: Additional information regarding the exam timetable and schedule.
4.0CBSE Class 10 Subjects
The subjects will be covered under the CBSE Class 10 Board curriculum. You must study all these subjects thoroughly to achieve good marks in your board exams.
5.0CBSE Class 10 Syllabus 2025
The CBSE board has issued the CBSE Class 10 syllabus for the academic year 2025. The syllabus is an important study resource for preparing for board exams. The syllabus for class 10th is divided into the following categories:
- Languages - (Group-L)
- Main Subjects - (Group-A1)
- Other Academic Electives -(Group-A2)
- Subject of Internal Assessment
It comprises the course structure, how the unit grades are distributed, and how many periods are allotted for each unit. Students can better organise their studies if they fully comprehend the CBSE Class 10 syllabus 2025. The CBSE board syllabus for Class 10 clarifies the subjects and concepts that must be learned in each subject. The question paper for the board exam is also created using the CBSE 10th syllabus.
6.0CBSE Class 10th Exam Pattern 2025
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) administers the Class 10 board exams for all CBSE-recognized schools. Students appearing for these exams should be well-versed in the format and updated annually to reflect the latest syllabus and student needs. Here are the critical details of the CBSE Class 10 exam pattern for 2025:
- Exam Structure: A single annual exam will be conducted.
- Marks Distribution: The exam carries a total of 100 marks, divided as follows:
- Board Examination: 80 marks
- Internal Assessment: 20 marks
- Passing Criteria: Students must score at least 33% in the board exam and the internal assessment.
- Question Types: The exam includes both objective and subjective questions.

For detailed information and to download the CBSE class 10th exam pattern 2025, visit the official website: cbse.gov.in.

12.0CBSE Class 10th Result 2025
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) usually announces the Class 10 results in the first week of May. Students can check their results on the official CBSE websites, cbse.gov.in and cbseresults.nic.in. After the results are released, students should collect their original mark sheets from their schools.
Schools will distribute the original mark sheets once the CBSE Class 10 Result is announced. Students can choose their academic stream from Science, Commerce, or Arts for the next two years. Students who are unsatisfied with their results can request verification and re-evaluation. CBSE is expected to launch the online application process for re-evaluation and verification of results in June 2025 (tentative).
13.0Class 10 Exams Preparation Tips 2025
The Class 10 board exam is a crucial phase in a student’s life, laying the groundwork for their future career and success. To achieve your goals, it is essential to have a well-structured preparation strategy for the CBSE Class 10 exams. Here are some critical tips for students preparing for the 2025 board exams:
- Focus in Class: Pay close attention during lessons and ensure you go through the entire syllabus sequentially.
- Understand Marking Schemes: Learn the chapter-wise marking schemes for each subject to prepare effectively.
- Organize Study Materials: Collect all necessary handouts and revision tools and keep them organized for easy access during last-minute reviews.
- Use Flashcards: Break down subjects into bullet points, graphs, lists, and formulae. Flashcards can be helpful for memorisation.
- Teach to Learn: Explain answers to someone else. This technique helps reinforce your understanding and highlights areas needing more focus.
- Practice with Past Papers: Solve previous years' CBSE Class 10 exam papers to improve problem-solving speed and time management skills.
- Take Breaks: Short breaks between study sessions are essential to avoid burnout and maintain focus.
- Health and Wellness: Maintain good health, eat nutritious food, and get sufficient sleep to keep your mind and body in optimal condition for studying.

JEE Advanced 2026: Dates, Syllabus, Eligibility, Pattern
Latest Update: IIT Roorkee Releases FAQs for JEE Advanced 2026. To help students understand the exam process and clarify common doubts, 94 Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) have been published on the official website. These FAQs provide detailed information on eligibility criteria, additional subject requirements, tie-breaking rules, category changes, and special quota provisions.
JEE Advanced is a prestigious national-level engineering entrance examination that provides a pathway for science enthusiasts to secure admission to their desired engineering colleges. Annually, around 2.5 lakhs of students from science backgrounds appear for this exam across the country, aiming to fulfil their dreams of studying in top engineering institutions.
The Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Roorkee announced that the JEE Advanced 2026 exam will be held on 17th May 2026 (Sunday). This Computer-Based Exam is conducted in a rotation by the seven prestigious IITs, which include IIT Kharagpur, IIT Bombay, IIT Roorkee, IIT Madras, IIT Kanpur, IIT Delhi, and IIT Guwahati. These institutions collectively organize and oversee the examination.
JEE Advanced Qualifying candidates have the opportunity to pursue Bachelor's degrees, Integrated Master's programs, and Bachelor-Master dual degrees. The offered degrees include BS, B.Tech, B.Arch, B.Tech-M.Tech dual degree, 'BS & MS dual degree,' integrated M.Sc., and integrated M.Tech.

3.0JEE Advanced Eligibility Criteria
The requirements for admission into IITs through JEE Advanced Examination include a qualifying exam, minimum grades, and more. Candidates will be disqualified if the JEE Advanced 2026 qualifying requirements are not met. It should be mentioned that JEE Advanced 2026 will only accept applicants who have passed JEE Main. All applicants carefully review the JEE Advanced 2026 eligibility criteria before submitting their application to see if they qualify for admission or not. Candidates must meet all requirements for JEE Advanced eligibility, including those relating to age, the number of attempts, qualifying exam scores, and more.
Here are the 5 criteria:
- JEE Main 2026 Scores - Candidates from all categories should be among the top 2,50,000 candidates in the JEE Main 2026 B.E/B.Tech. Paper 1.
- Number of Attempts - A candidate is allowed a maximum of two attempts in two consecutive years for JEE Advanced Examination.
- Prior Admission to IITs - Regardless of previous admissions, whether the applicant continued in the program or accepted an IIT seat in the past, the candidate will not be eligible for admission to an IIT.
- Age Limit - Candidates must be at least 18 years old and have been born on or after October 1, 2001. SC, ST, or PwD candidates must have a five-year age relaxation, so they should have been born on or after October 1, 1996.
- Class 12 Examination Appearance Requirements - Students who passed their high school in 2025 or 2026 are eligible to apply for the JEE Advanced 2026, with Physics, Chemistry, and Mathematics as mandatory subjects.
Eligibility Criteria for Foreigners
- Foreign national candidates must meet a distinct set of JEE Advanced 2026 qualifying requirements. Candidates who are not Indian citizens or whose parents are not from India are regarded as foreign nationals. Candidates who do not fall under the OCI/PIO criteria are counted as foreign nationals.
- As per the decision (dated February 03, 2023) passed by the Hon'ble Supreme Court of India in Writ Petition (Civil) 891 / 2021, OCI/PIO candidates who have secured OCI/PIO card before 04.03.2021 shall be considered as Indian nationals, for the purpose of admission to IITs.
- These Candidates MUST appear in the B.E./B.Tech. Paper of JEE (Main) 2026 and qualify to be eligible for JEE Advanced 2026.
- Organizing IIT provides the following instructions for overseas applicants who intend to take the JEE Advanced exam:
- No Reservations: As per the Government of India guidelines, foreign nationals are not eligible for reservations in any categories.
- Educational Qualification: Foreign nationals applying for JEE Advanced 2026 should have successfully completed their 10+2 level education.
- International Students in India: International students studying in India can take the JEE Advanced exam under the OPEN category if they have completed their class 12th board examinations with the required percentage.
- Parental Identification: Foreign nationals are required to provide identification for both of their parents.
- Seat Allocation: A portion of the available seats in the JEE Advanced program is reserved for foreign applicants, typically around 10%.
4.0JEE Advanced 2026 Application Form and Registration Process
After the JEE Main 2026 exam results, the application form for JEE Advanced 2026 will be released by the conducting IIT.
Qualifying candidates will have to register on the official website of JEE Advanced and then fill out the application form online. After submitting the JEE Advanced application form online, candidates will be required to pay the application fees online, with the last date to be announced by the authority.
The detailed steps for the registration and application process are as follows:
Registration Process:
- Visit the official JEE Advanced website.
- Register with JEE Main 2026 roll number, password, and security pin.
- Fill in personal details (name, parents' names, contact info, address).
- Choose up to eight preferred exam cities.
- Scan and upload a passport-size photo and signature.
- Verify and submit the application form.
Documents to be Uploaded in the JEE Advanced 2026 Application Form
Candidates will be required to upload the following documents while filling out the JEE Advanced 2026 Application form:
- Class X certificate or Birth Certificate (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- Class XII (or equivalent exam) Certificate (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- Category Certificate (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- An additional category Certificate (Optional) (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- PwD Certificate (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- Scribe Request Letter (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- DS Certificate (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
- OCI Card/PIO Card (PDF - 50 KB to 300 KB)
Please note that the certificate formats are available in Annexure II of the JEE Advanced booklet for 2026, which will be available on their official website.

7.0Exam Pattern of JEE Advanced 2026
ALLEN informs you that the JEE Advanced exam pattern changes annually for both papers. Candidates can refer below exam pattern to understand the differences between JEE Advanced Paper 1 and Paper 2.
JEE Advanced 2026 Exam Pattern
- The exam will be conducted in an online mode (computer-based test) only.
- There will be two papers, Paper 1 and Paper 2. Each paper will last 3 hours, and all candidates must take both exams. Hence, the total duration of the exam will be 6 hours.
- The exam will be in Hindi and English languages. Candidates can opt for either of the two.
- The marking scheme for the exam changes each year. Hence, instructions regarding the marking scheme will be mentioned in the “Instructions to Candidates” section of the exam paper.
- There will be three different sections in both papers - Physics, Mathematics and Chemistry.

Students must take mock tests regularly after completing the syllabus and practising questions, this will help them understand the exam pattern and assess their level of preparation. Mock tests also aid in gauging the exam's difficulty level and in developing speed, accuracy, and time management skills. Candidates must carry the admit card along with an ID proof to the exam center; otherwise, they will not be allowed to enter.
10.0JEE Advanced 2026 Answer Key and Response Sheet
After completing the JEE Advanced 2026 exam, provisional answer keys for Paper 1 and Paper 2 will be displayed on the official website. Candidates can verify their answers using these keys and also submit any challenges they might have. After considering these challenges, the final answer keys and the JEE Advanced 2026 results will be posted. Furthermore, candidates will have the option to view and download their responses for both Papers 1 and 2.
11.0JEE Advanced 2026 Result
The result of the JEE Advanced 2026 exam will be announced expectedly in June 2026.
It will feature candidates' individual marks, the combined score from Paper I and Paper II, and other essential particulars. In addition to these details, it will also indicate the candidate's rank in the Common Rank List (CRL) and category rank (if applicable). After the JEE Adv 2026 results are announced, candidates can obtain their scorecards, which are necessary for their participation in the JEE Advanced counselling process.
12.0JEE Advanced 2026 Expected Cutoff
JEE Advanced 2026 cutoff marks are the minimum marks that a student should secure to qualify for counselling. The cutoffs to take admission will be different for different colleges but the minimum qualifying cutoff is the same. The aggregate marks will be calculated as the sum of the marks obtained in Mathematics, Physics and Chemistry. Candidates must satisfy the subject-wise as well as the aggregate qualifying marks to be included in a rank list. It's important to note that the maximum mark for the JEE Advanced 2026 paper is 360.

The Joint Seat Allocation Authority (JoSAA) will conduct the common process online. In this process, seats across IITs, IIITs, NITs, and other government-funded technical institutes (GFTIs) will be distributed among the candidates. Participants in the Joint Seat Allocation process must have achieved a Rank in JEE Adv 2026. JoSAA is only conducted online. 6.0Benefits of Knowing JEE Advanced 2026 Syllabus
Most of us are well aware of the massive advantage that one can have after clearing the IIT JEE 2026 exam. One can start early preparation to excel in the examination by planning their time as per the JEE Advanced syllabus. Here are the benefits.
(i) Getting Familiar
If you already know the JEE Advance syllabus 2026, then you are on the right path. If you are beginning the preparation, there must be a lot that you need to figure out and get acquainted with. By gathering the syllabus, you can get familiar with the topics and start preparing for the exam accordingly.
(ii) Competitive Spirit
You know that JEE 2026 is a competitive exam, and it is essential to keep your competitive spirit up. One needs to put in the effort and hard work to excel in the examination. Thus, knowing the syllabus early will help you complete it on time. Once the syllabus is complete, it will provide you with the motivation to level up your competitive spirit.
(iii) No Missed or Left-out Topics
The most common problem with students who have not gone through the JEE advanced syllabus carefully is that they miss the topics. These topics or chapters could be the downfall if they appear in the exam with high weightage. Hence, having the JEE advanced 2026 syllabus pdf in hand and planning to cover every inch of the syllabus can ensure there are no left-out topics.
(iv) Last Minute Pressure
If a candidate knows the syllabus, then he/she doesn’t have to go through the last-minute pressure of confirming the completed and remaining topics. Once the syllabus is at hand, the candidate can plan the sections-wise studying by segmenting the JEE Advanced syllabus. They can easily prepare for all the topics and complete them way before the exam and revise again. This will help one relieve the pressure that many candidates go through at the time of the exam.

1.0What is NEET UG 2025 Counselling?
The Counselling process for NEET UG 2025, aimed at facilitating admission to MBBS and BDS courses, will be administered by the Directorate General of Health Services (DGHS) on behalf of the Medical Counselling Committee (MCC). This applies to the 15% All India Quota (AIQ) seats in government colleges and 100% seats in deemed/central universities, ESIC/AFMC, AIIMS, JIPMER, Delhi University (DU), BHU, and AMU.
The NEET 2025 counselling can be divided into two types:
- 15% All India Quota (AIQ) and
- 85% State Quota (SQ) seats
MCC conducts the AIQ counselling, while the respective State Counselling Authorities handle the counselling for SQ seats.
2.0NEET Counselling Rounds
The MCC counselling for AIQ seats will be conducted in four rounds:
- Round 1
- Round 2
- Round 3 (formerly called Mop-Up Round)
- Stray Vacancy Round
If seats remain vacant after these four rounds, additional rounds may be conducted.
3.0NEET Counselling 2025 - Overview
4.0 What after NEET-UG 2025 Result?
The candidate can complete two important tasks after the NEET 2025 result is announced:
- Calculate the NEET 2025 score
- Predict NEET 2025 rank using NEET rank Predictor
- Discover colleges that align with your NEET score
Given the trends of NEET admissions last year and comparing them, one can understand medical/dental colleges they may receive depending on their NEET rank and score. Candidates can estimate the NEET scores using the trend analysis of the NEET answer key 2025. Once the NEET scores are estimated, students can forecast their ranks based on NEET scores versus rank trends.
NTA releases the NEET 2025 answer key at -neet.nta.nic.in. The National Testing Agency will release the official NEET answer keys on the official website in PDF format along with the OMR sheet.
5.0When Will NEET 2025 Counselling Begin?
The regulatory body for NEET-UG exams, the NTA (National Testing Agency) conducts NEET in the offline mode (pen-paper test).

Eligibility Criteria for Central Universities
NEET 2025 Cut-off Percentile and Marks
Reservation of AIQ Seats in NEET 2025
In the NEET 2025 examination, a specific number of seats have been set aside for different categories within the 15% All India Quota. Candidates belonging to these specific categories will be considered for admission to these reserved seats under the All-India Quota, provided they meet the necessary eligibility criteria.
Please review the allocation of seats based on categories below.
8.0NEET 2025 Counselling Process
For NEET 2025 candidates aiming to take part in the Counselling procedure for All India Quota (AIQ) seats in Deemed Universities, Central Universities, and ESIC colleges, the initial step involves online registration via the official MCC website.
The subsequent NEET 2025 Counselling process is elaborated below:
NEET Counselling Registration
Step 1 – Sign Up as a New User
To begin the NEET UG 2025 Counselling process, candidates should follow the provided link. They will be asked to provide information such as their chosen stream (Medical or Dental), NEET UG roll number, application number, candidate's name, date of birth, and a security pin. After entering these details, they should click the 'Submit' button.
Upon submission, an agreement page will appear, giving aspirants the option to either 'Agree' or 'Don't Agree'. If candidates wish to proceed with the application, they must select 'I Agree'. At this point, login credentials will be displayed on the screen. Candidates should make a note of these credentials, including their password, and set up a security question for added protection.
Step 2 – Submission of Personal Information
During this step, applicants will need to furnish specific information such as the mother's name, the father's name, and the candidate's address. Once submitted, it is crucial to emphasise that these details are permanent and cannot be modified by the candidates later. Therefore, it is essential to ensure that the information entered aligns with the documents that will be presented during the admission process.
Step 3 – Pay the NEET Counselling Fees
Applicants must provide a non-refundable registration fee and a refundable tuition fee. These fees can be remitted through online methods, including credit card, debit card, and net banking.
The following table outlines the specific amounts of fees that candidates must submit.
Step 4 – Get Your Registration Confirmation
Once you've completed the submission of your details, a registration slip will appear on the screen. Be sure to print this slip, as it will be essential for the next steps in the admission process. To proceed, simply click on the 'Login' button.
Choices Filling
Step 5 – Available Choices
The 'Available Choices' link will become active once the Counselling process commences. On the left side of the screen, you will find ‘available choices’ option. Candidates who have paid the fees for both 15% All India Quota (AIQ)/Central Universities and Deemed Universities can apply for seats in both categories.
Step 6 – Pick your preference
- Candidates should use the '+' button next to their preferences to make selections. When a preference is chosen, it will shift from the left side of the screen to the right. After making their choices, candidates will have the option to save and continue by clicking 'Save & Continue,' or to save their selections and exit by clicking 'Save & Go to Home Page.'
- Candidates can also rearrange and remove their choices. The upward and downward arrows allow for shifting choices one step in the respective direction, and the delete option removes a selected choice. Additionally, candidates can click on a choice and drag it to switch places with another choice.
Choice Locking
Step 7 – Lock the Choices
Candidates participating in NEET UG 2025 Counselling must ensure that they finalize and lock their preferences before the specified deadline. Prior to locking, they have the flexibility to make changes to their choices. However, it's important to note that if candidates fail to lock their choices, the selections they have entered will be treated as final and will be automatically locked on the scheduled date.
Step 8 – Print Confirmation
It is recommended to print a copy of your selected preferences, as it can be advantageous for candidates during the subsequent stages of NEET UG Counselling. Even if candidates have not yet confirmed their choices, they can still generate a printout of their preferences once the choice-locking process concludes.
Step 9 – Seat Allotment
After each Counselling round, the NEET Counselling result will be announced, presenting the seat allocation details through a list and an allocation letter. This information will reveal which seats have been assigned to the candidates, including their name, roll number, designated category, candidate category, assigned college, quota, and course. The seat allocation letter holds significant importance, as it must be presented when reporting to the allocated college, along with the required documents.
Step 10 – Reporting to the Allotted College
When candidates arrive at the designated college to finalize their admission, they are required to present the following mandatory documents in their original form:
9.0Documents Required For NEET Counselling
Below is the list of documents required for the counselling process:
- NEET Admit Card Issued by NTA
- NEET Seat Allotment Letter Issued by MCC
- Date of Birth Certificate (if Matric Certificate does not bear the same)
- NEET Result/Rank Letter Issued by NTA
- Class X Certificate and Marksheet
- Class XII Certificate
- 8 Passport Size Photographs (Same as the one affixed on the application form)
- Identity Proof (Aadhar/PAN/Passport/Driving License)
NRI candidates are required to provide the following essential documentation:
- A copy of the sponsor passport along with an embassy certificate.
- A sponsorship affidavit, confirming the sponsorer's commitment to covering all study expenses throughout the entire duration of the program.
- An affidavit detailing the relationship between the candidate and the sponsorer.
- The SC/ST Certificate, as per the prescribed format mentioned in the prospectus/information bulletin, must be issued by the authorized body and should be in either English or Hindi. It is essential for the certificate to clearly indicate the sub-caste. Some states may require the certificate to be in English, and if the original certificate is in a regional language, the candidate should also have an attested translated copy in either English or Hindi.
- To avail the OBC reservation benefit, candidates must possess an OBC certificate issued by the authorized authority. The specific sub-caste mentioned in the certificate should align with the Central List of OBC. Additionally, it's crucial to note that OBC candidates should not fall within the Creamy Layer category to claim this reservation benefit. Furthermore, the OBC certificate must adhere to the standard format specified in the prospectus or Information Bulletin.
- A Disability Certificate must be obtained from a duly constituted and authorized Medical Board for individuals with 21 Benchmark Disabilities in accordance with the Rights of Persons with Disability Act 2016 (RPWD Act, 2016). For detailed information, please refer to clause 10.1 in the Information Bulletin of AIPMT/NEET, which is published by the Central
Board of Secondary Education (CBSE). It's important to note that no other certificate for persons with disabilities issued by any other Authority or Hospital will be considered valid. Candidates should also review the specific requirements of the college to which they have been admitted, as additional documents may be necessary.
Note: Candidates are advised to:
- Verify document requirements with the allocated college.
- Upload scanned copies of original documents on the MCC portal as per the schedule.
- Failure to submit original documents will result in admission denial at the Medical/Dental College.
10.0About NEET Counselling 2025
In reference to NEET 2025, the MCC NEET 2025 Counselling process plays a pivotal role in determining admissions to MBBS and BDS courses within the nation. To be eligible for admission to these medical programs, a passing score in the NEET exam is mandatory.
The NTA NEET Counselling can be categorized into two main types:
- All India Counselling for 15% AIQ Seats
- State Counselling for 85% State Quota Seats
NEET UG 2025 Counselling: For 15% AIQ Seats
The MCC is scheduled to administer four rounds of Counselling, which will consist of AIQ Round 1, AIQ Round 2, Round 3, and the stray vacancy round. Eligibility for participation in these rounds will be determined by candidates' NEET 2025 results, excluding those from the Union Territory of Jammu and Kashmir. In case there are unfilled seats following the second round of NEET Counselling in 2025 under the All-India Quota, these vacancies will be returned to the respective states.
The AIQ for NEET Counselling in 2025 will encompass the following:
- 15% MBBS/BDS Seats of States
- 100% MBBS/BDS Seats of BHU
- 100% MBBS Seats of AIIMS across India
- All India Quota of JIPMER (Puducherry/ Karaikal)
- All India Quota seats of AMU/ DU/ VMMC/ ABVIMS
- All India Quota Seats of Faculty of Dentistry (Jamia Millia Islamia)
- All India Quota Seats of ESIC
NEET UG 2025 Counselling: For State Quota Seats
In the context of NEET UG 2025 Counselling, it is worth mentioning that the Medical Counselling Committee (MCC) will hold the Counselling process for 15% All India Quota (AIQ) seats. In contrast, the rest of the 85% seats will be filled through the state-specific Counselling processes for NEET UG 2025, which will be conducted by each state separately. This will include the allotment of seats in private colleges within a state, which will also be done through the Counselling system of the respective state.
NEET UG Counselling 2025: What are the New Changes?
As the NEET UG 2025 exam has not yet been held, the official counselling rules are yet to be released. Nevertheless, aspirants can refer to last year's NEET UG counselling (2024) for an idea about the anticipated rules and format. The following are some of the main points from last year that will probably persist:
- No resignations allowed in the 3rd Rounds of AIQ/Deemed/Central Universities.
- OCI cardholders have equal eligibility rights as Indian citizens for NEET UG 2025 Counselling.
- Round 2 requires fresh registration for candidates who didn't register in Round 1 or weren't allocated seats.
- Non-reporting for Round 2 results in the forfeiture of security deposit.
- Fresh registration for Round 3 is necessary for candidates who didn't register in Round 1, Round 2, or resigned/not reported in those rounds.
- If a candidate joins the Round 2 seat, they can express willingness to upgrade to Round 3, but resigning leads to a security deposit forfeiture.
- Upgraded candidates must obtain relieving from their Round 2 seat to join Round 3, with no option for further upgrades or resignations.
- Non-joining in Round 3 results in exiting the NEET Counselling with a fee forfeiture and no participation in further rounds.
- Candidates allotted a seat in the Stray round must join the allotted seat/college; failure to do so leads to disqualification from the NEET UG exam for the next year, along with a forfeiture of fees.
