Directorate of Government Examinations (DGE), Tamil Nadu
The Directorate of Government Examinations (DGE), Tamil Nadu, is the state-level authority responsible for conducting and managing all public examinations in the state. It ensures fair and transparent evaluation for students studying under the Tamil Nadu State Board.
The DGE conducts the Secondary School Leaving Certificate (SSLC) and Higher Secondary (HSE) exams and also oversees several other government-level examinations. Established to maintain educational standards, DGE Tamil Nadu supports students, teachers, and schools by providing updated syllabi, resources, and timely results.
This guide will help you understand what DGE Tamil Nadu does, how your exams are held, and where you can find syllabus details and study materials.
1.0Quick Facts About DGE Tamil Nadu
- Full Name: Directorate of Government Examinations, Tamil Nadu
- Established Year: 1975 (functioning under the Department of School Education)
- Headquarters: DPI Campus, College Road, Nungambakkam, Chennai – 600006
- Official Website: dge.tn.gov.in
- Key Exams Conducted: SSLC (Class 10), HSE (Class 12), ESLC, Diploma in Elementary Education, NMMS, NTSE, and other scholarship exams
2.0History & Background of DGE
The Directorate of Government Examinations (DGE) was formed to bring all school-level public examinations in Tamil Nadu under one administrative body.
Earlier, exams were handled by separate agencies, but in 1975, the Tamil Nadu government established the DGE to unify and streamline the process.
Since then, DGE has been responsible for:
- Preparing exam timetables and guidelines
- Managing exam centres and question paper distribution
- Evaluating answer scripts
- Publishing results on time
Today, the DGE administers exams for nearly 25 lakh students every year across the state.
3.0Latest Updates / Notifications
- TN SSLC and HSE Exam Dates 2026: The Class 10 (SSLC) and Class 12 (HSE) exams are expected to be conducted in March–April 2026.
- Supplementary Exam Notification: DGE announced supplementary exams for students who couldn’t pass in the main exam; registration begins right after result declaration.
- Digital Access: The DGE website provides downloadable syllabus PDFs, blueprints, sample papers, and result links for both SSLC and HSE.
- Grading System: The board follows a grade-based evaluation for Classes 9–10 (A1, A2, B1, etc.) to reduce exam stress and promote concept-based learning.
4.0Role & Functions of the DGE
The Directorate performs several important tasks for the state’s education system:
- Conducting public examinations for Classes 10, 11, and 12 (SSLC, HSE I Year, HSE II Year).
- Prescribing exam patterns, blueprints, and assessment guidelines.
- Managing supplementary, private, and special category examinations.
- Ensuring secure printing and distribution of question papers across exam centres.
- Declaring results online via tnresults.nic.in.
- Issuing marksheets, certificates, and eligibility documents for students.
- Conducting scholarship exams like NMMS (National Means-cum-Merit Scholarship) and NTSE (National Talent Search Examination).
5.0Major Examinations Conducted by DGE
- SSLC (Secondary School Leaving Certificate) – Class 10:
- Annual public examination for students completing secondary education.
- Core subjects: Tamil, English, Mathematics, Science, and Social Science.
- HSE (Higher Secondary Examination) – Class 12:
- Final school-level exam that decides eligibility for higher education.
- Streams: Science, Commerce, Arts, and Vocational.
- Other Exams:
- ESLC: For private students who haven’t attended regular schooling.
- NMMS/NTSE: National scholarship tests for meritorious students.
- D.El.Ed. (Diploma in Elementary Education): Teacher qualification exam.
6.0Syllabus & Textbooks by DGE
The DGE Tamil Nadu follows the State Council of Educational Research and Training (SCERT) syllabus. This ensures that learning is conceptual, structured, and updated regularly.
The official textbooks are published by the Tamil Nadu Textbook and Educational Services Corporation and are available in Tamil and English mediums.
Students can download textbooks and syllabi from:
7.0Exam Pattern & Marking Scheme
Highlights:
- Each subject includes both objective and descriptive questions.
- Internal marks are awarded for assignments, tests, and practicals.
- Minimum passing marks: 35 in each subject (including theory and practical).
The marks distribution for the Class 10 Examination is given below:
8.0Important Dates & Exam Calendar
9.0Eligibility & Application Process
- Regular Students: Schools affiliated with the Tamil Nadu Board automatically register their students for public exams through DGE.
- Private Candidates: Those not enrolled in a regular school can apply directly on the official DGE portal by submitting documents (age proof, previous mark sheet) and paying the exam fee.
- Special Students: The board also provides separate exam facilities for students with disabilities or those under special categories.
Students must confirm their exam centres, hall tickets, and time schedules from their schools or via the DGE website before the exam.
10.0Resources for Students
- Previous Year Question Papers: Available online for SSLC and HSE for practice and pattern understanding.
- Model Papers: Released every year by DGE to help students familiarise themselves with new question formats.
- Answer Keys / Blueprints: Provided on the board’s website for self-evaluation.
- Preparation Tips:
- Prepare short notes while revising.
- Focus on understanding key concepts rather than rote memorisation.
- Practise at least one full paper weekly under exam conditions.
- Stay healthy and get enough rest before exams.

CBSE Class 9 Examination
The Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE) conducts the Class 9 examination annually. Class 9 is a crucial stage in the student’s life where a strong foundation of concepts is laid down for the higher classes. CBSE is a well-renowned board that imparts quality education to Indian students. It aims at a well-rounded, strong, and vibrant school education system that fosters excellence in all aspects of a student's life.
Students should be aware of preparation tips and syllabi when preparing for CBSE Class 9. Keep reading for more information on the CBSE 9th exam details.
1.0CBSE Class 9 Subjects
CBSE prepares a syllabus for each of the subjects to make a strong base for the student in order to grab the exact concept that is very much needed in the higher classes. There are mainly core subjects in the class 9 syllabus which is listed below
Other elective subjects that depend on the school as extra options include Information Technology, Physical Education, or a foreign language. Each subject’s curriculum is designed to enhance specific skills and make smooth transitions into class 10.
Students can also check for class 9 printed study material available at ALLEN estore to strengthen their preparation. Designed to help you understand complex topics and concepts in an easier and effective way. The more your practice, the better you are able to understand concepts!
2.0Latest CBSE Class 9 Syllabus
The curriculum of the CBSE Class 9 is structured in such a manner that will lay the basic fundamentals for Class 10. The CBSE Board follows the NCERT curriculum, which is mandatory for all affiliated schools.
The New Education Policy has also integrated coding and computational thinking for students from class 9 to build technical knowledge among students as part of 21st-century skills.
For efficiency, students should pick those subjects to take in Class 9 that they intend to take in Class 10.
3.0CBSE Class 9 Exam Pattern
The CBSE Class 9 Exam Pattern outlines the format in which the question papers are designed, including the number of questions, marking scheme, and exam duration. Understanding this pattern is crucial for systematic preparation.
Note - Internal Assessment is a Total Mark of 20 for each subject which is divided into different categories based on the school choices:
- Project Work: Assignments and projects related to different subjects.
- Class Participation: Activeness in classroom activities and discussions.
- Oral Tests: Oral tests to assess reading, speaking, and listening skills.
- Notebook Submission: Regularity and neatness in notebook submissions.
4.0Recommended CBSE Class 9 Books
For effective CBSE Class 9 exam preparation, learning from NCERT textbooks provides a solid foundation of all basic concepts, so students should read the NCERT thoroughly line by line in order to achieve good marks in the examination.
Additional Reference Books for Practice: Expand your knowledge and sharpen your skills with these additional reference books, which Topper’s Students have widely used for comprehensive practice.
- Mathematics: RS Aggarwal, RD Sharma
- Science: Lakhmir Singh & Manjit Kaur
- Social Science: Arihant, Oswal
5.0CBSE Class 9 Preparation and Strategy
Below are some tips that will ease the preparation of any student who is going to appear in the Class 9 exam, which in turn will give them more confidence to appear for the final exams.
- Plan Smart: Draw out a balanced study timetable and focus on strong, as well as weak subjects.
- Distraction-Free Zone: Move your study to a no-noise area.
- Visual Aids: Maintain notes, flowcharts, and diagrams to help in simplification of concepts.
- Practice Papers: Solve past year’s papers to realize the pattern of questions that come in the exam and how to manage time.
- Mock Test: Take mock tests regularly for self-evaluation.
- Early Revision: Complete the syllabus a month prior to the examination so that you revise everything.
- Take breaks: Relax your mind with small breaks in between study sessions.
6.0List of Olympiads for Class 9
After students have completed their Class 9 exam, they will be promoted to Class 10. However, there are other competitive exams too that students can appear for. These exams test a student’s intellectual, critical thinking, conceptual, and problem-solving skills. Below is the list of exams that students can take after their CBSE Class 9:

- International Drawing Olympiad (IDO)

- International Maths Olympiad (IMO)
- English International Olympiad (EIO)
- National Essay Olympiad (NESO)

IIT-JEE Maths
Mathematics is the science of structure, order, and relationship, growing out of some basic practices like counting, measuring, and describing shapes. Logic insistence and quantitative calculation of a particular task are basic criteria that are pivotal for success in the JEE examination. Much of mathematics has become very abstract with time; this evolution is of paramount interest while dealing with problems in JEE.
In many cultures, mathematics also evolved well beyond simple counting, driven by such varied needs as commerce and technology. This creation is all the more necessary for JEE aspirants since it is based on the intricate problem-solving required in the examination.
Mathematical systems, such as Euclidean geometry—a core part of JEE—are built on axioms and theorems. Understanding these logical foundations is vital for mastering mathematics at the JEE level.
1.0Introduction
Mathematics is the study of patterns, structure, and relationships, rooted in fundamental practices like counting, measuring, and analyzing shapes. It combines logical reasoning with quantitative calculations, evolving from practical applications to highly abstract concepts. Over centuries, mathematics has become essential in various fields, from physical sciences and technology to life sciences, playing a crucial role in understanding and solving complex problems.
2.0IIT JEE Maths Study Material
IIT JEE Maths Important Topics - JEE Main and Advanced
The Maths syllabus for JEE Main and JEE Advanced are almost similar. When compared with Main, Advanced is more concept-based. Students need in-depth understanding of topics to answer the questions.
Students can check the below links and access detailed information about the different Maths topics:
Also Read:
JEE Advanced Mathematics Syllabus
3.0Branches of Mathematics
4.0The Scope of Mathematics
Mathematics is a very broad and diversified field, reaching much further than simple arithmetic and geometry; it comprises branches such as algebra, calculus, statistics, and number theory—subjects of prime importance in JEE preparation. The physical sciences, engineering, and increasingly the social and life sciences build upon these branches. For JEE preparation, students need to get a grip on problem-solving at both practical and theoretical levels.
5.0Applications of Mathematics
Mathematics is not confined to theoretical studies; it has practical applications in numerous fields:
- Science and Engineering: Mathematics is fundamental in understanding natural phenomena and designing technology.
- Economics and Finance: Mathematical models and statistics are used to analyze markets, optimize resources, and manage risks.
- Medicine and Biology: Biostatistics and mathematical modeling help in understanding diseases, medical imaging, and genetics.
- Computer Science: Algorithms, cryptography, and data structures rely heavily on mathematical principles.
- Everyday Life: Mathematics helps in budgeting, cooking, planning, and problem-solving in daily activities.

Social Olympiad
Social Olympiad typically refers to competitive examinations or competitions related to social studies or social sciences. These events aim to encourage students' competitive aptitude, showcase their intellectual abilities, and promote excellence in the field of social sciences. The specific format and focus of Social Olympiads may vary depending on the organising body and region.
Being a part of the Olympiad exams like the Social Olympiad is not merely about competing; it is about the enormous value and learning that accompany it. You may be an aspiring historian, an emerging economist, or a politics buff; the Social Studies Olympiad provides a single platform where you can broaden your vision and mingle with fellow minds.
1.0About Social Science Olympiads
Social Olympiads are competitive events designed to challenge and evaluate students' knowledge and understanding of the social sciences, which include disciplines like history, geography, economics, political science, and sociology. Social Olympiads aim to deepen students' knowledge of the social sciences and develop critical thinking and problem-solving skills.
2.0What is the National Social Studies Olympiad (NSSO)?
Indian Talent Olympiad hosts the National Social Studies Olympiad every year. It tests the knowledge of the students in civics, geography, and history. This test is conducted within schools and replicates what a student learns during class. All students, whether they study through the state board, CBSE, ICSE, or the International Board, can take it.
3.0NSSO 2025-26: Important Dates and Events

Note: These dates for the online exam are expected. The definitive exam schedule will be provided 20 days before the exam dates.
4.0How to Register for the Social Science Olympiad?
Registering for a Social Science Olympiad typically involves steps that may vary slightly depending on the specific Olympiad you're interested in. Here's a general guide on how to register:
- Identify the Olympiad: First, determine which Social Science Olympiad you want to participate in, such as the International Geography Olympiad, International Economics Olympiad, or any other.
- Check Eligibility Criteria: Confirm that the participant fulfils the eligibility criteria for the selected Olympiad.
- Visit the Official Website: All relevant registration information is typically found on the Olympiad's website.
- Registration Process: Registration can be done online through the Olympiad's website.
- Payment of Registration Fee: If a registration fee is applicable, make the payment as guided on the website.
- Preparation: After being registered, the participants should start preparation. The website of the Olympiad generally offers study material, sample papers, and other resources.
5.0Smart Preparation Tips for Excelling in Social Studies Olympiad
It is crucial to keep these Social Studies Olympiad preparation tips in mind. They are the cornerstones for planning, staying informed, utilizing resources effectively, and maintaining discipline, all of which are pivotal for success.

Taking part in Social Olympiad exams offers students numerous advantages, enhancing their academic career and personal growth in several ways:
- Students acquire concepts, past happenings, and ongoing concerns outside the usual curriculum of social science subjects.
- Students apply collecting, evaluating, and combining information in preparation for exams through extensive study.
- These tests test students to think critically, evaluate arguments, and analyse information, all of which are skills that are required in academics and elsewhere.
- Preparation and competition can build confidence, resilience, and pressure-handling ability, which can lead to personal development overall.
- International Olympiads make students learn more about international issues and views, fostering greater comprehension of cultures and interconnectivity.

Social Science - Classes 6 to 10
Social Science is the field that looks into the human experience, ranging from relationships with other people to the complexities in societies and cultures. It encompasses various disciplines, including economics, geography, political science, history, sociology, psychology etc. In short, social science is important because it helps us understand ourselves, our societies, and the world around us.
Social science is crucial in helping students appreciate their culture, history, and language. This subject fosters creativity, critical thinking, and ingenuity in students and contributes to their development as well-rounded individuals.
1.0What is Social Science?
Social Science is the gamut of all disciplines that study human behaviour, societies, and cultures. In other words, unlike the natural sciences that deal with the physical world, social science delves into the intricacies of human relationships, institutions, and civilizations.
From uncovering the stories of ancient civilizations to grasping the systems that shape modern societies, it lays the groundwork for becoming thoughtful, informed, and responsible citizens. Whether you're a curious Class 6 learner or diving deeper into concepts in Class 10, this platform is your gateway to exploring the fascinating worlds of History, Geography, Civics, and Economics.
In the following links, we will discuss various concepts, the importance of geography, economics, political science, and history, and the benefits students can gain from studying these subjects.
2.0Major Fields in the Social Science - Classes 6 to 10
- Geography: This is the study of the Earth's surface. It deals with physical features, climate, and human populations. The subject also covered human-environment interactions, urbanization, and regional development.
- Economics: Economic study refers to how societies will go about producing, distributing, and consuming different types of goods and services, including topics around supply and demand, markets, and economic growth.
- History is the study of past events and their effects on the present. Historical figures, civilizations, and important events are analyzed, which helps understand the present and shape the future.
- Political science studies government, political systems, and political behavior. It also examines matters such as political theory, international relations, and comparative politics.
Subject Covered
Social science covers a wide range of fascinating subjects that are deeply connected. Here's a sneak peek at what students from classes 6 to 10 will dive into:
History
- Uncover captivating stories of human civilizations.
- See how past events have shaped the world we live in today.
- Explore intriguing topics like ancient cultures, medieval societies, colonialism, and modern revolutions.
Geography
- Discover the Earth's physical features, natural resources, and ecosystems.
- Learn how humans interact with their environment.
- Delve into climate, population, industries, and sustainable development topics.
Civics (Political Science)
- Understand democracy, government structures, and citizens' rights.
- Study the Indian Constitution, elections, and governance in society.
- Explore the workings of global organizations and international cooperation.
Economics
- Grasp the basics of production, consumption, and distribution.
- Learn about resources, markets, and economic policies.
- Examine concepts like sustainable development and the global economy.
3.0Class-Wise Breakdown of Topics
Class 6 : Introduction to early civilizations, physical geography, and the basics of democracy.
Understanding rural and urban livelihoods.
Class 7 : Research medieval history, natural vegetation, and how governments work.
Explore human-environment interaction.
Class 8 : Study modern history, the Industrial Revolution, and the Indian freedom struggles.
Dive into resources and industries and understand the Indian judiciary system.
Class 9 : Study the French Revolution, world history, and climate systems.
Study democracy, constitutional design, and poverty as a challenge.
Class 10 : Focus on globalization, sustainable development, and resource management.
Study the rise of nationalism, world wars, and India's freedom struggle.
4.0Scope of Social Sciences
Social science offers important insights about the human condition. It also endows us with the knowledge and skills required for living in this complex, integrated world.
- Understand Human Behavior: Social science helps us understand how people act, how societies operate, and which factors are capable of altering societal patterns.
- As part of studying social sciences, this leads to the development of critical thinking, analysis, and the ability to view information from various perspectives.
- It fosters global citizenship, requiring people to learn about various cultures, societies, and political systems, which gives us a deeper understanding of the world and more responsibility worldwide.
- Researching in social science allows us to understand different social issues about poverty, inequality, or environmental challenges; it helps build a solution from there.
By connecting people, places, and ideas, social sciences offer a comprehensive understanding of the world and help drive positive change.
5.0Importance of Social Sciences
1. Encourages Critical Thinking
Social sciences teach us to ask questions, analyze situations, and interpret information. These skills help individuals think logically and make well-informed decisions in everyday life.
2. Builds Social Awareness
By exploring societal issues like inequality, poverty, human rights, and environmental challenges, social sciences help people understand the world's problems and inspire them to take action for positive change.
3. Cultivates Global Citizenship
Learning about different cultures, histories, and systems fosters empathy and respect for diversity.
4. Prepares Future Leaders
Social sciences equip individuals with the knowledge and skills to lead. Whether in governance, education, or community development, these insights empower people to create a more inclusive society.
5. Addresses Real-World Challenges
Collecting data, analyzing trends, and conducting research, social sciences tackle pressing issues like urbanization, climate change, unemployment, and public health crises.
6. Drives Economic Progress
Fields like economics analyze how resources are managed and policies are created. This contributes to sustainable economic growth and ensures fair resource distribution for all.
7. Strengthens Democracy and Justice
Social sciences promote democratic values and justice by teaching civic responsibilities, the rule of law, and legal systems, ensuring fairness and equality in society.
In essence, social sciences are vital for understanding and improving our world, empowering individuals to create meaningful change and contribute to a better future.
Keep learning and stay tuned with ALLEN Digital for the latest CBSE exam updates, sample papers, last year's question papers, study materials, etc.

  Title  : Biology
  URL    : https://allen.in/neet/biology/

NEET Biology
Biology, as the study of living organisms, explains the structure, function, growth, and change of life. Biology also includes the classification of living things, how living organisms interact, and the distribution of life on Earth. In summary, Biology encompasses the study of everything from cells and molecules to ecosystems and the biosphere as a whole.
When it comes to NEET Preparation, Biology is one of the most significant subjects to focus on, partly due to the fact that it is one of the easiest subjects to achieve a high score on because many questions will often have direct concepts from NCERT. So, if you have a good understanding of Biology, it can add reasonable marks to your total score and thus improves your chance of achieving a good NEET rank.
Therefore, students preparing for NEET should focus on different important topics and study strategies. With a lot of practice, good notes, and revision, Biology can become one of the most scoring subjects and help you perform well on the exam.
1.0NEET Biology Study Material
Botany and Zoology Important Topics:
2.0Branches of Biology
Biology is a diversified field, with many branches dealing with different aspects of living organisms and their relationships with their surroundings. However, in spite of these specializations, all living things share common biological activities like reproduction, cell division, and genetic heritage.
Learning all of these branches of Biology is important for NEET preparation, as they make up a substantial portion of the syllabus prescribed for students of classes 11th and 12th.
Zoology
Zoology is the branch of Biology that deals with animals and animal life. It involves studies on their structure, behaviour, physiology, classification, and ecological development. Zoology is one of the core topics in the syllabus of NEET and has many specialized sub-branches.
Given below is the list of topics in Zoology of prime importance for the NEET Preparation.
Botany
This study focuses on understanding various aspects of plant life, including growth patterns, reproductive methods, metabolic processes, and classification within different plant groups.
The table below provides a concise overview of key topics in NEET botany, offering a clear understanding of each area's significance.
Also Check:
3.0Importance of Biology
Biology, the study of life and living organisms is integral to understanding the natural world. It exerts an impact on many fields, such as the environment, technology, health, and agriculture.
1. Understanding Life Processes:
The processes of life are understood through it, including the central biological processes of genetics, evolution, metabolism, and cell function. Knowledge of these is indispensable for medical advancement and the formulation of new treatments.
2. Environmental Conservation: Through the study of ecosystems and biodiversity, Biology helps interpret the ways in which organisms depend upon one another and their environments to set a basis for sustainability and climate change mitigation.
3. Education and Research: Biology promotes scientific inquiry and critical thinking, giving students a wide range of career opportunities in environmental science, biotechnology, health-related professions, research, and teaching.
4. Agricultural Improvements: Biological advances in agriculture have been realized by developing crops that are pest-resistant and high-yielding, promoting sustainability, and increasing productivity.
5. Medical Advancements: Biology is at the base of knowledge about diseases, developing cures, and creating vaccines. It underpins breakthroughs in genetics, gene therapy, and personalized medicine.
4.0Applications of Biology
Biology has a wide range of applications that impact our daily lives, health, the environment, and technological advancements. The following are some important uses for biology:
1. Medicine and Healthcare: Biology plays a vital role in identifying disease mechanisms. In this line, it has led to the preparation of vaccines and the development of vaccines, treatments, and diagnostic tools. Genetic engineering and gene therapy have revolutionized medical science, and this could be used for the treatment of various genetic disorders.
2. Agriculture and food Science: It makes available genetically modified and selectively bred crops for growing drought-tolerant, pest-resistant, and high-yielding crops to ensure food security, hence reducing the need for chemical pesticides. It improves animal husbandry practices, enhancing quality and efficiency in dairy and other animal products.
3. Environment and Conservation: Biology thus helps manage ecosystems, control pollution through biotechnology, restore habitats, and conserve endangered species. It also contributes to the production of biofuels and carbon sequestration in the fight against climate change.
4. Biotechnology and Industry: Biology underlies biotechnology which results in innovation in pharmaceuticals, agriculture, and environmental science. Biotechnology applies biological systems to develop new products and technologies that solve various problems in industries.
5. Forensic Science: Biology is critical in forensic science, where DNA fingerprinting is used in criminal investigations and the detection of paternity. Biological samples, such as blood, hair, and tissues, are therefore vital in forensic evidence.
- Education: School biology prepares a foundation for scientific literacy, critical thinking, and awareness about issues of biology, providing a platform for science practitioners and informed citizenship.

ICSE Class 7 Exam Overview: Syllabus, Sample Papers, and More
ICSE Class 7 by the Council for the Indian School Certificate Examinations (CISCE), is an important year in your educational journey. It establishes a solid foundation towards higher classes like Class 8 as well as prepares you, in a gradual manner, for your ICSE Board years in the future.
Even though the exams for ICSE Class 7 are conducted by your school, they will follow the same syllabus pattern and teaching standards according to the CISE.
1.0Key Features & Highlights of ICSE Class 7
2.0ICSE Class 7 Syllabus & Curriculum Structure
The ICSE Class 7 syllabus is designed to strengthen your basics in every subject. The concepts are taught in detail by connecting to real-life examples.
Subject Structure:
- Core Subjects: English (Language & Literature), Second Language, Mathematics, Science, Social Science (History, Civics, Geography, Economics)
- Optional / Enrichment Subjects: Computer Applications, Environmental Education, Art, Physical Education, Moral Science, Performing Arts, etc.
Core Syllabus Highlights of ICSE Class 7
The brief outline of what the syllabus includes for ICSE Class 7 is given below.
- English Language & Literature: Grammar (tenses, clauses, punctuation), comprehension, essay/letter writing, and literature lessons including stories and poems.
- Mathematics: Integers, fractions, decimals, ratio and proportion, simple algebra, geometry (lines, triangles, circles), mensuration, and data handling.
- Science (Physics, Chemistry & Biology):
- Physics: Motion, energy, light, sound, and basic machines.
- Chemistry: Elements, compounds, mixtures, acids, bases, and simple reactions.
- Biology: Cells, tissues, plants, animals, human body systems, and health.
- Social Science (History, Civics & Geography):
- History: Early civilizations, Indian dynasties, Mughal Empire, etc.
- Civics: Rights, duties, local government, and values of citizenship.
- Geography: Continents, climates, natural resources, and map skills.
- Computer Applications: Basics of computers, input/output devices, data storage, software, and simple coding concepts.
3.0ICSE Class 7 Exam Pattern & Grading System
Each ICSE Class 7 school prepares its own question papers, but the structure generally follows the CISCE assessment guidelines.
- Theory Paper: Usually includes short answer, long answer, and application-based questions.
- Internal Assessment: Covers class tests, homework, projects, practicals, and regular assignments.
- Passing Criteria: Most schools require at least 33-35% in each subject to move to the next class.
4.0ICSE Class 7 Study Materials & Resources
To prepare well for the ICSE Class 7 exams, use the right study materials and practice resources.
- Textbooks: Follow the books prescribed by your school based on the CISCE syllabus.
- Sample Papers & Question Banks: Practise ICSE Class 7 sample papers and old question sets to understand the question pattern and improve time management.
- Reference Books / Selina Series: Books like Selina Concise for Mathematics and Science are often recommended for extra practice.
- Notes & Revision Guides: Prepare your own notes for key concepts and formulas for quick revision.
5.0ICSE Class 7 Preparation Tips for Students
Here are some simple and effective preparation tips for ICSE Class 7 exams:
- Understand: Focus on understanding the concepts especially in Science and Maths.
- Create a Study Routine: Study a little every day instead of last-minute cramming.
- Revise Weekly: Keep Sundays or weekends for revising what you’ve already studied.
- Use Diagrams & Mind Maps: These help you remember topics easily.
- Practise Writing: Write full answers for English and Social Studies questions.
- Be Active in Class: Ask questions, take notes, and complete homework regularly.
- Take Care of Yourself: Eat healthy, get enough sleep, and stay relaxed during exams.
Disclaimer: The information provided above represents a commonly followed structure across many ICSE-affiliated schools. However, the exact syllabus, textbook list, marks distribution, assessment methods, paper pattern, internal assessment activities, weightage, and evaluation criteria may vary from one school to another. Students are strongly advised to verify all details, including syllabus topics, exam format, and prescribed books directly with their teachers or school authorities.
Frequently Asked Questions

NEET 2026 Eligibility Criteria
NEET Eligibility Criteria 2026 - An Overview
Are you ready to embark on your journey towards becoming a medical professional in 2026? The first step is understanding the NEET UG eligibility criteria, and we've got all the vital details right here!
The National Testing Agency (NTA) has provided the essential NEET UG 2026 eligibility criteria for aspiring medical students. Prospective applicants must familiarize themselves with these requirements, accessible in the official NEET 2026 brochure on the website, to ensure a smooth application process. Failing to meet any of these criteria will result in disqualification from the entrance test.
1.0NEET UG Eligibility Criteria 2026 - Key Highlights
Academic Qualification | 10+2 or its equivalent with Physics, Chemistry, and Biology. |
NEET Eligibility Marks | General Category – 50% (PH – 45%) OBC/SC/ST – 40% (PH – 40%) Note: Mathematics scores are not considered for this examination. |
Age Limit | Minimum: 17 Years Age Maximum: No upper age limit |
Number of Attempts | No restrictions on the number of attempts. |
Nationality | Indian Nationals NRIs, OCIs, PIOs & Foreign Nationals |
Score Validity | One Academic Year |
2.0Academic Qualification for NEET 2026
Candidates should pass their 10+2 or equivalent exam with individual passing grades in Physics, Chemistry, Biology/Biotechnology, and English.
3.0NEET Eligibility Criteria Category Wise
NEET eligibility criteria is crucial for all aspiring medical students. Here, we provide a detailed breakdown of the eligibility requirements, categorized to help you easily identify the criteria applicable to your specific category.
Category | Aggregate marks required for NEET 2026 |
Unreserved | 50% |
SC/ST/OBC/Reserved-PH | 40% |
Unreserved- PH | 45% |
4.0NEET 2026 Eligibility Age
The NEET (National Eligibility cum Entrance Test) 2026 eligibility criteria, particularly concerning the age limit.
Category | Minimum Age Requirement | Maximum Age Requirement |
Unreserved Category (UR) | Born on or before December 31, 2009 | No Longer Applicable |
SC/ST/OBC |
5.0Eligibility Criteria - Number Of Attempts
In 2018, the NEET UG eligibility criteria removed any restrictions on the number of attempts allowed for the NEET exam. As a result, there is no limit to the number of times a student can take the NEET(UG) exam, provided they meet the eligibility requirements.
6.0Eligibility Criteria for NRI/Foreign Students
Foreign-educated candidates seeking eligibility for NEET must meet certain requirements. They should have completed an examination equivalent to a Class 12 qualification in their home country. Additionally, they must have studied Physics, Chemistry, Biology, and passed both the theoretical and practical assessments in these subjects, along with passing an English examination in their qualifying education.
- Admission to Medical and Dental Colleges is open to Non-Resident Indians (NRIs), Overseas Citizens of India (OCI), and Foreign Nationals, but it is subject to the specific regulations set forth by the respective State Governments, educational institutions, and the Government of India.
- NRIs and OCI applicants must include a Certificate issued by the relevant Indian Diplomatic Mission in their country of residence to verify their NRI/OCI status when submitting their online application for NEET UG 2026.
- Similarly, foreign applicants who wish to take the NEET 2026 exam should provide documentary evidence, such as pertinent pages from a valid passport or a Certificate from the competent authority in their home country confirming their citizenship status when submitting their online NEET application.
7.0Validity of NEET UG Scores
The NEET score you will obtain in 2026 is valid for a single year. This enables candidates who successfully pass the NEET 2026 examination to seek admission into undergraduate medical and dental programs during the academic year 2026-2027.
It is essential to note that while the NEET score remains valid for this duration, it does not serve as a guarantee for admission to a specific institution or course. To secure admission, candidates are required to actively participate in the NEET 2026 counseling process and fulfill the unique eligibility criteria set by individual colleges and universities.
8.0NEET (UG) 2026 Qualifying Codes
Qualifying Exams for NTA's NEET UG 2026:
Code 01: Candidates who are planning to take the NEET exam in 2026 and are awaiting the results of their Class 12 board exams must select this code. However, they must ensure they meet the specified criteria for Class 12 qualification by the time of admission, as failure to do so will render them ineligible.
Code 02: Aspirants preparing for the higher/senior secondary examination or the Indian School Certificate Examination (ISCE), which is equivalent to Class 12 and involves 12 years of study, should opt for this code. According to the National Council of Education Research and Training (NCERT), the last two years of this study should include science subjects like Physics, Chemistry, Biology/Biotechnology, and Mathematics or other elective subjects, with English as a core subject. This code also applies to "droppers" - candidates who have passed their qualifying examination from CBSE/ICSE or any state board in a previous year but wish to appear for NEET 2026.
Code 03: Aspirants who have completed their Intermediate/Pre-degree Examination in Science from an Indian University/Board or a recognized body, including Physics, Chemistry, Biology/Biotechnology, and compulsory English with practical tests in science subjects, should choose this code. This code is also applicable to candidates who completed their Class 12 or its equivalent from a state board.
Code 04: Aspirants who have cleared a Pre-professional/Pre-medical Examination after passing the Higher Secondary Examination or Pre-University or an equivalent examination with Physics, Chemistry, Biology/Biotechnology, and English as a compulsory subject must select this code. The Pre-professional/Pre-medical examination should involve practical tests in these subjects.
Code 05: Candidates pursuing a three-year degree course from a recognized University, with Physics, Chemistry, and Biology/Biotechnology as mandatory subjects, and who have passed the first year with practical tests in these subjects, should use this code. It's important to note that the qualifying exam here should be a University Examination, and candidates should have previously passed their 10+2 with Physics, Chemistry, Biology/Biotechnology, and English as a core subject.
Code 06: Those who have passed the B.Sc. Examination from an Indian recognized University with at least two of the subjects - Physics, Chemistry, Biology (Botany, Zoology)/Bio-technology, along with English, must choose this code. They should have also passed their earlier qualifying examination (10+2) with English, Physics, Chemistry, and Biology.
Code 07: Aspirants who have cleared an examination, whether from India or a foreign country, that is deemed equivalent in scope and standard to the last two years of 10+2 study, comprising Physics, Chemistry, and Biology/Biotechnology, including practical tests, must select this code.
Stay connected with ALLEN for all the necessary information regarding the NEET 2026. CBSE Syllabus and Exam Pattern
Are you a student aspiring to get top marks for your exam? Or a parent trying to find ways to better support their child for their exam? Or maybe you are a teacher preparing your lesson plan for the upcoming year? Either way, understanding the syllabus and exam pattern set by the CBSE is the first step towards academic success.
Every year, the curriculum and exam format for Classes 1 to 12 are updated by the Central Board of Secondary Education (CBSE). For the academic year 2025-26, the curriculum has been updated and is live on the CBSE websites. Understanding it will help you stay organised, keep track of your progress, and prepare smarter.
1.0CBSE Curriculum: What It Includes
Your CBSE curriculum is like your subject-wise guidebook. You can see it as a boring list of all the chapters, or you can consider it as a full plan that includes the following:
- Clearly defined learning goals
- Chapter-wise and unit-wise content
- Assessment structure (theory as well as practical)
- Project guidelines
The new and updated CBSE Syllabus is not about rote memorisation anymore. It has shifted to practical understanding and competency-based learning. This has made the syllabus smarter, lighter, and tailored for success.
2.0CBSE Exam Pattern 2025–26
Yes, knowing what to study is important. But understanding the exam pattern is just as important. If you don’t know what to expect for your exams, here is a simplified version:
This format has been created to ensure that, instead of just mugging up facts, students are not trying to comprehend the syllabus.
3.0Class-wise Syllabus and CBSE Curriculum
The CBSE provides a class-wise syllabus for all subjects from Classes 1 to 12. Students can click on their respective classes to access detailed syllabus PDFs:
CBSE Class 12 Syllabus
The final chapter of school life! The CBSE exam syllabus for Class 12 is rigorous, combining theory, practicals, and internal assessments. The year’s goal? Board success and entrance exam excellence. The learning focus for this is revision mastery, mock tests, and exam writing precision.
CBSE Class 11 Syllabus
Senior secondary starts here. Students select streams: Science, Commerce, or Humanities, and dive deep into specialised subjects. The CBSE curriculum for Class 11 also aligns with entrance exam prep. For this class, students have to focus on advanced reasoning, project work, career planning, and more.
CBSE Class 10 Syllabus
The pressure is real, and so is the support! With the CBSE syllabus for Class 10 in hand and clear exam patterns to follow, students can prepare confidently for their first board exams. Your learning focus for this class should be on time management, revision strategies, and solving as many sample papers as you can.
CBSE Class 9 Syllabus
Class 9 lays the groundwork for board exams. The CBSE exam syllabus for Class 9 starts to take shape with formal assessments, internal evaluations, and lab activities. You must master the concepts, practice lab experiments, and work on answer-writing techniques.
CBSE Class 8 Syllabus
Class 8 is the dress rehearsal for high school. The CBSE syllabus for Class 8 introduces abstract ideas and exam-style assessments. Some schools also offer Artificial Intelligence or IT as optional subjects. It prepares you with exam readiness, structured writing, and academic discipline.
CBSE Class 7 Syllabus
In the Syllabus of CBSE Class 7, students go beyond basic understanding. They participate in science activities, learn about governments and economies, and solve more complex mathematical problems. This is the year of practical application, independent research, and collaboration.
CBSE Class 6 Syllabus
Welcome to middle school! The CBSE syllabus for Class 6 now includes formal Science and Social Science, with concepts explained in greater detail. This year is all about getting students used to subject-specific study. The focus now needs to shift to deeper analysis, critical thinking, and time management.
4.0Benefits of Knowing the CBSE Syllabus and Exam Pattern
- Plan Like a Pro: Knowing what is coming is the best way to prepare smarter. When you use the CBSE syllabus, it will be easier for you to organise your study routine from day 1. There is no more guesswork.
- Zero In on What Really Matters: Not everything in your textbook has the same value. Some concepts will have more weightage while others will have less. When you know this, you can focus your energy on relevant stuff that actually carries marks.
- Revise Better, Worry Less: When you walk into an exam knowing what to expect, you are more likely to write clear, accurate answers. A well-used syllabus makes sure that your revisions are targeted and effective.
- Make NCERT Your Best Friend: NCERT books have been designed as per the CBSE curriculum. If you stick to these books, you will be well-prepared for your exams.
- Get a Head Start on Competitive Exams: If you are planning to crack JEE, NEET or CUET, finishing the CBSE curriculum is a good place to start. Whatever advanced concepts you need to study for these competitive exams are actually built on the core concepts you can find in the NCERT books.
5.0How to Download CBSE Syllabus PDF
If you want to download the CBSE syllabus PDF, here is what you need to do:
- Visit cbseacademic.nic.in, the official CBSE website.
- Click on “Curriculum 2025-26.”
- Select your class and subject.
- Download the PDF.
Frequently Asked Questions

The NEET UG 2025 scorecard will include the candidate's subject-wise marks, total marks, percentile score, All India Rank (AIR), and qualifying status. 5.0How to Check NTA NEET 2025 Result
Aspirants can check their NEET-UG Result 2025 online following below steps:
- Visit NTA official website(s): nta.ac.in/ or neet.nta.nic.in/
- Click on the “NEET UG 2025 Result” link.
- Enter your application number, DOB, and security pin.
- Click on “Submit” to view your result.
- Download the NEET 2025 scorecard and take a printout for future reference.
Detailed mentioned on NEET Scorecard
- Candidate's roll number
- Application number
- Personal details (including candidate’s name, father’s and mother’s name, gender, DOB, nationality, category/sub-category, etc.)
- NEET AIR (All India Rank)
- NEET qualifying status
- Percentile score
- Percentile obtained subject-wise:- Physics, Chemistry, and Biology (Botany and Zoology)
- Total Marks scored
- NEET AIR for 15% AIQ seats
- NEET Cutoff Score
6.0What After NEET 2025 Result?
The NEET 2025 result will make a significant milestone for lakhs of medical aspirants across India. But the real journey begins after the result — from counselling to college selection and admission. After the results, qualified candidates must:
- Register for All India Quota Counseling (MCC).
- Apply for state quota counselling. Each state conducts separate 85% State Quota Counseling via its respective medical authority.
- Explore private/deemed universities if eligible.
- Use NEET college predictor tools to estimate MBBS seat chances.
- Keep the following documents ready for counselling: NEET 2025 Admit Card & Scorecard, Class 10 & 12 Marksheet, Passport-size photographs and Identity proof (Aadhaar, PAN, etc.), Domicile Certificate (for State quota), Caste Certificate (if applicable).
Candidates who do not qualify in NEET 2025 can explore alternative career options such as BDS, AYUSH courses (BAMS, BHMS, BUMS), Nursing, or B.Sc. medical and paramedical programs.
